#7002. Maura Higgins Seemingly Responds to Harsh Fan Feedback After Her “Toughest” Week on ‘DWTS’

“Some weeks will be better than others, and that’s okay. I’m only human,” she wrote, in part.

Maura Higgins is opening up after tackling her Disney Night choreography as Tinker Bell on Dancing With the Stars. The Love Island alum got candid in a lengthy statement she penned alongside a carousel of behind-the-scenes photos from Monday night’s episode after fans criticized her performance in Team Slaytopia’s group number.

“This week was definitely one of the toughest I’ve had so far. I was really unwell, running on very little energy and trying to learn two dances, but I gave it absolutely everything I had,” she started her message, adding that she had no previous dance experience before joining DWTS.“I wanted to challenge myself, step outside my comfort zone and give something completely new my absolute best & that’s exactly what I’m trying to do every single week. I’m learning, I’m making mistakes, I’m pushing myself and I’m growing along the way!! Some weeks will be better than others, and that’s okay,” she continued, adding, “I’m only human.”She insisted she wasn’t sharing the message for “sympathy or to make excuses, but simply to share a little of the reality behind the scenes.”

The Traitors star uplifted her pro partner and “biggest support throughout this journey,” Mark Ballas, before seemingly addressing harsh feedback from viewers who criticized her “low energy” performances in both of her numbers this week.

“Genuinely what is up with maura tonight…she was the clear weak link of that team dance,” one person wrote, while another added, “i'm going to be very honest with you i genuinely think maura did by far the worst of any celebrity tonight and the judges were just distracted by how good of a show mark is able to put on that they didn't notice lol.”

Per her message, Maura seems to be focusing on those who continue to uplift and support her throughout the competition.

“To everyone who continues to support me, encourage me and cheer me on, please know how much it means,” she wrote toward the end of her statement. “You genuinely give me the strength to keep going, especially on the harder days. Your kindness never goes unnoticed.”

She then closed her post by expressing how “grateful” she is to still be in the running for the iconic Mirrorball trophy.

Based on that alone, I tink she’ll be just fine either way!

#6997. The 15 Best Moisturizers Perfect for Mature Skin

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Nourishing favorites from brands like Augustinus Bader and ZIIP come expert-recommended

There are plenty of ways to treat signs of aging, but among the most effective is also the simplest: Striking the right moisture balance for your skin. Proper hydration can help reduce the appearance of fine lines and dullness, while also improving skin laxity over time. While almost any face cream can boost hydration, the best moisturizers for mature skin provide targeted, preventative treatments in a jar.

Key ingredients, such as peptides, retinoids, and others, work together to target specific concerns. “Retinoids or plant-based alternative bakuchiol improve and prevent fine lines, increase cellular turnover, improve skin texture and elasticity, and [reduce] discoloration,” dermatologist Morayo Adisa tells Bazaar. “Look for antioxidants such as vitamin C and E to help with collagen production and decrease oxidative stress. Hyaluronic acid, glycerin, and urea to increase skin hydration, softening the appearance of lines and wrinkles. Also, look for an emollient such as squalane or shea butter.”

Several moisturizers feature at least one of these transformative ingredients, so how do you know which one is right for you? To help narrow down your options, we asked Adisa, along with other dermatologists, for their thoughts on some of the best moisturizers for mature skin.

Neutrogena continuously proves that you don't have to spend a ton of money to reap the benefits of wrinkle-smoothing ingredients. This night cream targets the primary causes of wrinkles, uneven tone, and loss of elasticity with a firming and plumping lipid complex called Hexinol, brightening vitamin C, and moisturizing ingredients such as shea butter and glycerin.

Key ingredients: vitamin C, glycerin, hexinol

The clinical studies on this gel cream after four weeks of use are pretty impressive. Co-signed by dermatologist Hadley King, this moisturizer works at a cellular level to restimulate collagen production, diminish dark spots, and significantly smooth deep-set wrinkles.

“It contains humectants and emollients like squalane, as well as niacinamide, which improves tone and texture, and willowherb, which has senolytic properties to help promote skin longevity,” says King. The brand's proprietary combination of plant stem cells targets exosomes, energizes skin through NAD+, and decreases inflammation.

Key ingredients: squalane, niacinamide, willowherb, plant stem cells

Bring the spa home: This do-it-all hydrator makes it a breeze to give mature skin a refresher. Valmont’s Prime Renewing Pack leaves skin with a healthy glow in minutes and uses a creamy formula that reduces fine lines and wrinkles while seamlessly smoothing texture. Use a thin layer of the Pack as a quick five-minute mask, or a thicker layer for a more intensive treatment.

Key ingredients: peptides, RNA liposome, triple DNA complex

Sisley Paris's Black Rose cream is a go-to choice for beauty lovers and makeup artists alike, thanks to its excellent priming capabilities. The star ingredients, alkekengi calyx extract and padina pavonica, work in tandem to plump skin that's lost volume. There's also hibiscus flower extract and alpine rose extract, which help erase dullness, making your skin look more vibrant and glowy before you apply any makeup.

Key ingredients: black rose oil extract, black rose aqueous extract, alpine rose extract, alkekengi calyx extract, padina pavonica extract, hibiscus flower extract, shea and camelina oils

If you’re looking for a longer-lasting alternative for skin hydration, allow us to introduce you to Neuraé’s Harmonie The Sleeping Mask. The nourishing mask is created to rejuvenate dull, dry skin while reducing redness over time. Plus, its emollient-rich formula is the perfect addition to your beauty routine in colder months.

Key ingredients: gardenia jasminoides extract, shea butter, kokum butter, babassu oil

Here’s a top-rated moisturizer from Sulwhasoo that minimizes dullness, fine lines, wrinkles, and dryness simultaneously. The hypoallergenic cream has a whipped texture that works for a range of skin types and delivers up to 24 hours of hydration.

Key ingredients: Korean herb extract, Jisun firming complex, white mulberry, jujube, Chinese skullcap

Augustinus Bader is another worthy splurge, and in a few uses, you'll understand why. The Rich Cream utilizes a proprietary blend of TFC8, which features squalane, argan, sunflower seed, and evening primrose oils to stimulate cellular renewal and deliver targeted ingredients to skin cells.

Key ingredients: evening primrose oil, argan oil, sunflower seed oil, squalane, glycerin

If you’re already a fan of ZIIP’s devices, this moisturizer from the brand deserves a place in your rotation. The brand’s high-tech essential works with your skin’s electrical activity, along with ingredients like encapsulated collagen peptides, argireline, and more, to smooth expression lines while boosting firmness and radiance after six weeks of use.

Key ingredients: collagen peptide moisturizer with argireline amplified peptide solution, marine samphire bioactive

The 2:4:2 in the names refers to the trifecta of two percent ceramides, four percent cholesterol, and two percent fatty acids that work together to rebalance the skin and even out elasticity irregularities that cause sagging, roughness, and enlarged pores. It's a deeply moisturizing formula that replenishes and seals in moisture to help skin look and feel smooth and plump.

Key ingredients: ceramides, cholesterol, fatty acids

You'll see impressive skin-firming results with many of the moisturizers on this list, but this one is specifically designed for those who don't want to wait weeks to reap the benefits of their cream. Over time, the formula stimulates the sirtuin protein within the skin to boost collagen, hyaluronic acid, and elastin production.

Key ingredients: instant tightening sugars, micro-hyaluronic acid, glycerin, sunflower seed oil, grapeseed oil, shea butter

Dermatologist Marisa Garshick notes that some moisturizers contain ingredients that help support collagen production, thereby improving the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles while firming the skin. “A great option is the Mother Science Molecular Genesis barrier repair moisturizer, which uses their patented ingredient malassezin to strengthen the skin barrier, while also containing other ingredients like hyaluronic acid, growth factors, copper peptides, lipids and squalane, which together help to boost hydration, reduce the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles, and offer antioxidant benefits against free radical damage,” she explains.

Key ingredients: malassezin, copper peptides, squalane, hyaluronic acid, lipid concentrate

Mature skin can still experience breakouts, but this formula, which King also recommends, replenishes lost moisture without clogging pores or making the skin oilier. The formula looks to dimethicone for moisture and adaptogenic Chinese herbs to strengthen compromised skin barriers.

Key ingredients: dimethicone, ginseng, gotu kola, dongquai

Another one of Adisa's top choices is this rejuvenating cream from Andalou Naturals. The formula utilizes bakuchiol, a gentler yet equally effective alternative to retinol. “Bakuchiol works to reduce the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles and overall leaves the skin feeling rejuvenated,” Adisa explains. It also features a fruit stem cell complex and super antioxidants to restore and protect the skin barrier. "This has a nice, lightweight whipped consistency that quickly absorbs," notes Adisa.

Key ingredients: bakuchiol, resveratrol, goji berry, aloe vera

This rich moisturizer, created by dermatologist Ben Johnson, is rich in barrier-protecting oils and antioxidants. The cream also contains an effective blend of phosphatidylcholine, raspberry seed oil, and shea butter to keep skin nourished. However, if you require even more moisture, consider layering the brand's face oil, Osmosis Nourish, on top after application.

Key ingredients: phosphatidylcholine, raspberry seed oil, shea butter, apricot oil, blueberry

Here's a top-rated cream that works overnight to smooth and tighten as you snooze. The formula not only evens skin's texture but also repairs its barrier while visibly lifting sagging skin. Plus, since you won't need more than two pumps of the favorite to cover your entire face, a little goes a long way.

Key ingredients: retinol, bakuchioil, vitamin C, hyaluronic acid, antioxidants

Adisa noted earlier that retinoids or the plant-based alternative, bakuchiol, are ideal for improving and preventing fine lines, increasing cellular turnover, smoothing skin texture, and enhancing elasticity—so check ingredient lists for these. Garshick says to also look for moisturizers like hyaluronic acid and glycerin, along with ingredients to support the skin barrier, such as ceramides and squalane, and peptides or growth factors to help with firmness. “Antioxidants like vitamin C can help to protect against environmental stressors, while niacinamide may be added to help calm and soothe the skin,” Garshick notes.

“As we age, the skin barrier weakens, contributing to increased moisture loss, and there is decreased production of sebum, hyaluronic acid, and ceramides, key ingredients that help to keep the skin moisturized. This makes the skin more prone to dryness and dullness,” Garshick explains.

Johnson adds that skin dries out with age primarily due to a loss of circulation. “As the skin ages, it loses about one percent of circulation each adult year, so at 50, the skin has 25 percent less circulation,” says Johnson. “This leads to fewer nutrients, lipids, and immune cells, which result in a porous, dehydrated barrier.”

Incorporating a moisturizer for mature skin helps hydrate the skin while supporting the skin barrier. “Some moisturizers may also contain ingredients that help to support collagen production, improving the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles while also firming the skin,” notes Garshick.

Johnson advises steering clear of alpha-hydroxy acids because they can dry out and damage the skin. Ceramides, while generally good for sealing in moisture, can also be problematic. “They have a feedback-loop effect on the skin that results in them slowing down your epidermal turnover rate,” the expert says. You should also ease off on harsh exfoliants, which can damage the skin barrier and speed up moisture loss, leaving skin dry and crepey.

#6990. Taylor Swift Reveals the Cult Film Character She and Longtime Friend Abigail Have Been Impersonating Since They Were Teens

“It’s something our husbands have to put up with”

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Taylor Swift loves her pop culture references. After all, she named two of her cats after Grey’s Anatomy and Law & Order: SVU characters, her songs feature allusions to everything from Peter Pan to Romeo and Juliet, and she once even brought Lisa Kudrow on stage to do a rendition of “Smelly Cat” from Friends.

So, it comes as no surprise to learn that Swift is a secret fan of one of the biggest cult classic comedies of the early 2000s: Napoleon Dynamite. As it turns out, the movie was one of the first things that she and her longtime friend, Abigail Anderson, bonded over when they were teenagers.

This tidbit was revealed when Swift sat down for a rare Q&A with Toy Story 5 director Andrew Stanton yesterday afternoon, following a screening of the film in New York City. During their conversation, Swift noted how the relationship between the characters Bonnie and Blaze (voiced by Scarlett Spears and Mykal-Michelle Harris, respectively) mirrors that of her and Anderson’s friendship.

“I’d love to pile on to what Andrew was saying about finding someone who’s the same genre [of] weird and silly as you are and just why Bonnie and Blaze’s story resonated with me so much,” Swift shared.

“When I moved to Nashville when I was 14... you know, you’re the new kid in school and you’re like, ‘Who should I be friends with? These girls are cool, these girls are cool, these are nice girls,’” Swift recalled. “But there was one girl named Abigail—and she even has curly hair, too, like Blaze—and the two of us, all we wanted to do was talk in Napoleon Dynamite voices.”

Swift went on to reveal that this playful bonding exercise has continued long past their high school years. “And we still do this,” she said with a laugh. “And it’s something our husbands have to put up with.” (Swift is married to NFL tight end Travis Kelce, while Anderson is wed to a chief technology officer named Charles Berard.)

The two have been friends ever since they met in English class at Hendersonville High School in Tennessee, and Swift has referenced her BFF in songs like “Fifteen” and “Ruin the Friendship.” Anderson has even appeared in a number of Swift’s music videos, for tracks like “Picture to Burn” and “I’m Only Me When I’m With You.”

After sharing her special anecdote, Swift went on to do an impression of the titular Napoleon Dynamite character. Then, she tied it all back to Toy Story 5 by saying: “When you find that person who just completely sees you for the strange, little genre that is you at your purest, that hit me so hard watching the film.”

#6985. What Does Non-Acnegenic Mean, Exactly? Dermatologists Break Down the Skincare Term

And I thought I knew all the acne buzzwords...

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

When I was a young girl in the early 2000s, there was always a bottle of that iconic blue Cetaphil Gentle Skin Cleanser in my parents’ house. It was probably the only thing I washed my face with for the first 10 years of my life. But what stuck with me the longest (and perhaps was an early sign that I was destined to be a beauty editor) was that, right under the product name, the label said “non-comedogenic.” This new word perplexed me. I vividly remember trying to figure out how to pronounce it as I washed my face every evening, finally asking my mom during my very short nighttime skincare routine. She told me how to say it and what it meant in a way that a 7 year old would understand, and that was that. I was all up to speed.

Fast forward two decades later, and I’m sitting here, perplexed once again, as I learn about a new-to-me, related-to-non-comedogenic term: non-acnegenic. I’ve been seeing the word pop up here and there when it comes to an acne-friendly skincare routine, but I had to know, what actually makes it different from non-comedogenic? Is it a marketing buzzword, or is whether or not a product is non-acnegenic really important to pay attention to? I had far more questions than 7-year-old me, so I turned to two dermatologists to explain everything.

Let’s start with some good news: The definition of non-acnegenic is honestly pretty straightforward. “Non-acnegenic means a product has been formulated and tested so it won’t trigger acne breakouts, including the red and inflamed bumps and pustules most people picture when they think of acne,” says board-certified dermatologist Mona Foad, MD.

If you have acne-prone skin and/or are currently trying to figure out how to get rid of acne, Dr. Foad calls it “one of the most useful labels you can look for.” This is because brands “typically test these products on people with acne-prone skin over several weeks with a dermatologist evaluating the skin for any new breakouts,” she explains.

However, using a non-acnegenic skincare product isn’t a 100 percent guarantee that it won’t cause a breakout, unfortunately. “Think ‘less likely to invite a breakout,’” says board-certified dermatologist Mona Gohara, MD. This label means the product is unlikely to cause or worsen acne, but there’s no way for a brand to promise that result for everyone.

Non-acnegenic and non-comedogenic are different—but there’s plenty of overlap, Dr. Gohara says. Non-comedogenic, as you may already know, means that a product “is designed not to clog pores,” says Dr. Foad, who notes that a comedone is the technical term for a clogged pore. “Non-comedogenic addresses clogged pores, while non-acnegenic addresses the full picture of acne, including inflammation.”

Here’s what I found really interesting: “A product can avoid clogging pores and still irritate the skin enough to trigger inflammatory breakouts,” says Dr. Foad. Just because a product is non-comedogenic doesn’t mean you won’t get a breakout after using it. But that doesn’t mean using a non-comedogenic product can’t be helpful in avoiding acne, either. “Clogged pores are the starting point for acne, so preventing them is the first line of defense,” Dr. Foad explains. “When a product carries this label, the brand is claiming that its formula won’t block your pores and set the stage for breakouts.” Think of it this way: Acne-prone skin wants protection from potential pore cloggers and acne triggers alike. So, if you’re shopping for acne-prone skin, Dr. Foad recommends looking for products that are both non-comedogenic and non-acnegenic.

Oh, and another important thing to note: “Neither means the product actually treats acne,” says Dr. Gohara. Remember, these labels just mean that you’re putting skincare or makeup on your face that is less likely to set off a breakout.

As discussed above, non-comedogenic and non-acnegenic do have accepted definitions in the beauty industry. But there’s one super important caveat: Just like any non-medical claim on an over-the-counter beauty product, neither of these terms is regulated by the FDA. “This is one of the biggest sources of confusion I see,” says Dr. Foad. Because the FDA doesn’t define or regulate either of these, “there’s no standard test brands are required to use,” she says.

What does that mean, exactly? “One company might test a product on dozens of acne-prone volunteers over several weeks, while another might rely on ingredient lists alone,” explains Dr. Foad. “That’s why these labels are a helpful starting point, not a guarantee.” Your best bet is to look for brands that are transparent about their clinical and consumer testing, so you can review those and decide for yourself if a product looks promising enough to buy.

There’s no one magical ingredient that automatically makes a product non-acnegenic, but these are often used in products meant to support acne-prone skin:

Again, the inclusion of these ingredients doesn’t guarantee you won’t break out after using them. If you have an allergy or sensitivity to anything on this list, you should avoid it.

On the flip side, here are some ingredients to avoid, as they can trigger breakouts and clogged pores:

In general, for those who are acne-prone, “I’d steer them away from heavy, oil-based foundations, concealers, and facial balms,” says Dr. Gohara. Instead, look for lightweight foundations and other makeup products that don’t contain the ingredients above.

Of course, anyone who’s acne-prone, oily, or combination may want to ensure their beauty products are non-acnegenic. But beyond that, “they’re also essential for patients using prescription acne treatments like tretinoin or benzoyl peroxide, which can be drying and require a moisturizer that won’t undo their progress,” says Dr. Foad.

While pimples are often associated with teenagers, you may also benefit from non-acnegenic products as you age: “I also see many women dealing with hormonal acne in their 30s and 40s, and through perimenopause and menopause, when breakouts can appear for the first time in years,” she adds.

#6983. The Best Dressed Celebrities at the CFDA Awards

All decked out in their favorite American designers

At most awards shows that we cover—the Oscars, the Golden Globes, the Grammys—fashion is a secondary concern for the judges and nominees. At the CFDAs, however, the entire evening is dedicated to the American fashion industry, and, much to our annual delight, the attendees always dress with that in mind. Last night’s wardrobe selections were not dominated by European fashion houses (or celebrity contracts). Instead, guests seized the opportunity to celebrate American talent, much of which is based here in New York, with collections equally worthy of a spot on the red carpet.

Take, for instance, Mona Tougaard in a sweet, colorblocked look from Lii by Zane Li, who was nominated in the emerging category this year. Or actress Sydney Lemmon, who wore a long-sleeved, button-front black sheath worn just undone enough to reveal a pair of red, bedazzled panties underneath, created by Emily Dawn Long, another emerging nominee. And then there was returning nominee—and winner!—Colleen Allen, who outfitted guest Gracie Abrams in a strapless, twisted, black velvet gown.

Those who represented the larger American brands leaned into a more relaxed approach to dressing. Emily Ratajkowski wore a simple black sweater paired with a low-rise orange skirt and black boots from Tory Burch. Elle Fanning showed up in a dusty pink slip beneath a floor-length leather trench from Coach by Stuart Vevers. (Each was nominated for awards: womenswear and accessory designer, respectively.)

And then came Anthony Vaccarello, nominated for International Designer of the Year, swanning in from Europe with his high-glam, gold-foiled posse. Hailey Bieber, Zoë Kravitz, Rosé, and Anja Rubik each wore a look from his megawatt Spring 2027 collection, which was almost entirely blanketed in gold. But they still managed to retain some semblance of casual American ease with bomber jackets layered over knee-length skirts and bedazzled, midriff-baring two-piece sets.

All in all, it was a very well dressed celebration of the country’s fashion industry, so picking our favorites was difficult. But ahead, we’ve rounded up the best-dressed guests of the 2027 CFDA Awards.

In archival Bob Mackie, Levian earrings, and a Zydo Italy ring

Rosé, Anja Rubik, Anthony Vaccarello, Zoë Kravitz, and Hailey Bieber

#6976. Vince Staples Is Our November 2026 Music Director

The rapper crafts an exclusive playlist for Bazaar that ranges from songs from his childhood to songs that show the vastness of what hip-hop can be

Vince Staples is known for his matter-of-fact, bluntly honest lyrics. The California-born rapper often taps into the realities of Long Beach, his hometown, meditating on urban poverty, systemic racism, and even skepticism of the music industry. This lyricism emerges from the rapper’s desire to remain authentic and has been evident throughout his career from his 2015 debut, Summertime ’06.

“Whatever your viewpoint on life is or whatever you’re experiencing at this current moment is going to translate into the music that you create, no matter what,” Staples told Bazaar in an interview this summer. “As you get older, you learn to embrace that.”

The musician, 33, did exactly that on his seventh studio album, Crybaby, an urgent, politically charged record that trades traditional beats for post-punk melodies. He confronts historical racism on songs like “Tulsa, OK” and “Cotton” while indicting the entertainment industry for continuing to incite racism against Black people in songs like “TV Guide.” Other songs tap directly into his experience, like “White Flag,” which reflects on his personal journey as an artist and entertainer.

“I want to come up with the idea that feels most organic,” he says, adding that he never listens to music when he’s in the writing process. “I don’t like to be inspired by other music,” he says. “I feel like it’s a disservice to the lives that we live every day…. Music reminds you of what you experience in your life, and it forces you to look inward.” This same instinct guided Staples as he assembled the playlist for Bazaar’s Performance issue, compiling songs that have been foundational to him as an artist and performer.

He often admires introspective songwriting. Blu’s track “Vanity,” featured on the California rapper’s album Her Favorite Colo(u)r (2011), reminds him of the personal, diaristic storytelling style that influenced his early works. “The way he tells stories, it’s the way that I really got into hip-hop music for myself,” he recalls. Today, he cites his friend Steve Lacy’s ability to capture a “good adaptation of his thoughts at the current moment” with “Nice Shoes,” the lead single from the album Oh Yeah? Staples, reflecting on how personal music is, suggests that songs “all have to mean something to us for them to even come to mind” in the first place.

Given the artist’s relationship to music, Staples chose songs that reminded him of moments in his life. One of the album’s oldest tracks is Ray Charles’s soulful “Drown in My Own Tears,” which hearkens back to his childhood. “[Ray Charles] has been ever-present in my life because growing up as a Black person in America and Southern California with older parents or older grandparents, he would be a fixture in music, whether it’s country music, soul music, R&B, music—you can put him in a lot of those different subgenres, but just as far as black music in general,” he says.

Another staple of American Black music is James Brown, but Staples decided to include the Who’s muscular 1965 cover of Brown’s “I Don’t Mind.” For him, the song offers a lesson in transformation and reinterpretation, telling Bazaar it “unearthed the idea of coverage and the ecosystem of sharing music or repurposing music.” On his new album, for instance, he samples Slick Rick’s “Children’s Story” on his song “The Big Bad Wolf.”

In this way, Staples inherently views music as an expansive medium. He recalls how recently the Beastie Boys song “Sabotage,” a buoyant mixture of rap and hardcore punk, helped him explain to a friend “the vastness of what we consider to be hip-hop music.” Ultimately, for Staples, a song’s ability to change you makes it indelible. “It’s a testament to what it meant to you in the moment you first heard it,” he says. “Having that brought back to the forefront is a cool part of the process.”

Reminder: This season was filmed a year ago...things change!

#6971. Hailey Bieber, Zoë Kravitz, and Rosé Match in Metallic Gold to Honor Anthony Vaccarello at the CFDA Fashion Awards

Their looks were pulled from Vaccarello’s final collection at Saint Laurent

Anthony Vaccarello was feeling the love from three of his strongest disciples on Monday evening, as he was honored with the International Designer of the Year prize at the 2026 Council of Fashion Designers of America (CFDA) Fashion Awards in New York City.

The prize served, in part, as a tribute to Vaccarello’s 10-year tenure at Saint Laurent, during which he found quite a few famous devotees. So, it felt fitting that three of them—Hailey Bieber, Zoë Kravitz, and Rosé—bestowed him with the honor, all while matching in metallic-gold looks pulled from his final collection at the French fashion house.

About a week before Vaccarello announced his departure from Saint Laurent, he presented his Spring/Summer 2027 ready-to-wear show at Paris Fashion Week. Held in front of the Eiffel Tower, the swan-song runway show was defined by a stream of nearly 60 glittering gold looks, broken up by only a few outliers in black and bronze.

Pulling from this collection, Bieber wore a sheet metal-like midi dress, which was embroidered with beaded floral motifs, with spiky brown fur decorating the cuffs. A split ran up the center of the skirt, and the Rhode founder paired the design with matching strappy heels.

Kravitz, meanwhile, wore her own midi design in a crinkly material that emulated crocodile skin and featured a teeny belt string tied into a bow along the waist. She matched the dress with a pair of decaying chandelier earrings, but she added a pop of contrast with some grape-colored slingbacks (with gold-dipped toes, of course).

Finally, Rosé switched things up a bit by wearing a two-piece set consisting of a cropped sleeveless top and a split-hem midi skirt, both decorated with intricate beaded designs in a fabric similar to Bieber’s look. The top was trimmed with large golden studs that framed the piece like armor, and everything was matched with a pair of shiny gold sandal heels down low.

Watch below as the trio presents Vaccarello with his special honor at the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards.

#6970. Was There a Fall on ‘Dancing With the Stars’ Disney Night, or Was It on Purpose? Dancer Diego Pasillas Speaks Out!

Dancing With the Stars delivered a truly epic series of performances during Disney Night, including a packed opening number complete with a Newsies segment. And the whole thing was so well-choreographed that one of the dancers fell over and recovered so flawlessly that everyone thought it was on purpose.

While there was some debate from fans online, turns out this fall was, in fact, a fall. Dancer Diego Pasillas confirmed as much on Instagram, saying “You guys. I just ate it on national television.” He then did an example of the correct move, recreated his fall, and said “the show must go on.”

Diego’s comments were filled with praise and support from his fellow Dancing With the Stars cast members, including Hailey Bills, who wrote “BUT THE WAY YOU GOT UP,” and Val Chmerkovskiy who commented, “Bro you shablammed it and then shamblammed it in the air.”

Reminder: pro Adele Zaikman had to unexpectedly skip Disney Night due to being sick, and The Sun reports that her absence “left producers and dancers racing to rework multiple performances,” with the rehearsals “becoming even more chaotic” since she was booked for three different dances.

“She had to drop out very last minute, so that mixed with the week already being one day shorter has been really hectic behind the scenes,” a source said. “Multiple troupe members are going to fill in for her. The opening number was the big one. The other two she just had a tiny part, so it wasn’t as big of a deal to fill. But the troupe has been practicing all Monday before showtime.”

In other words, this was a slightly last-minute routine without a lot of practice time!

#6966. The “Longevity Manicure” Is the New Quiet-Luxury Treatment That Will Save Your Nail Health

Why experts love this technique for your strongest, healthiest nails ever

Nail health has quickly become central to a professional manicure. Cumulative care, safe gel removal, and prioritizing long-term strength over quick aesthetic fixes are now standard, alongside high-shine, chip-free finishes. This new philosophy stems from restorative nail treatments taking over salons, from precision dry techniques like the Russian manicure to strengthening builder gels, likely driven by the wider beauty movement toward skin-barrier protection, regenerative treatments, and customized routines.

"The future of manicures isn’t just about the latest color trends or how long your gel polish lasts, but how well we care for the nails underneath," says Krisztina van der Boom, founder of DryBy. "The longevity manicure is the answer to that. It's about beautiful nails today, without compromising the nails you'll have tomorrow."

Rather than viewing nail care as temporary color, this manicure takes a skin-first approach, treating the nail plate and surrounding skin as just as important as the polish itself.

“Think skincare versus makeup; a traditional manicure is the makeup, the beautiful, glossy finish, while a longevity manicure is about everything underneath,” van der Boom explains. “It's all about looking after the natural nail."

Where a conventional gel appointment might focus more on buffing and thick gel overlays to mask imperfections, a longevity manicure uses tailored, restorative techniques. Examples include keratin treatments to rebuild weakened nails, LED red-light therapy to boost collagen and cellular renewal in the hands, integrating hand care into your routine, and microexfoliation techniques that clean the cuticle without damaging the surrounding delicate tissue.

To understand how a longevity manicure works, consider the full life cycle of a nail appointment. "We think of a manicure in four stages: preparation, application, maintenance, and removal," van der Boom says. "Traditionally, most of the attention goes into the first two—achieving that perfect, glossy, long-lasting finish. But what happens afterward matters just as much."

The core philosophy is to eliminate harsh mechanical buffing and aggressive chemical soak-offs. "Some of the most avoidable nail damage happens not when a manicure is applied, but in how it's worn and removed. That's why we place so much importance on thoughtful preparation, precision techniques, responsible maintenance, and gentle removal."

To do this, salons are drawing inspiration from advanced skin health technologies and incorporating high-tech tools and barrier-strengthening formulas into the treatment steps.

"It's about bringing the same thoughtful, preventative approach to our hands and nails that we've come to expect from facial skincare," notes van der Boom.

Whether your nails are naturally brittle, damaged from years of harsh gel removals, or simply need a healthier routine, this approach suits all nail types and needs. "The longevity manicure is designed as an ongoing ritual rather than a one-off treatment," van der Boom says. “With consistent, thoughtful care, the aim is to preserve the integrity of your natural nails, minimize avoidable damage, and support the skin of your hands over time.”

She believes this is all about prevention rather than correction. "You can disguise unhealthy nails with a beautiful finish, but there's nothing chicer than beautifully groomed, healthy nails even when they're completely bare."

Cuticle Hydration: Apply cuticle oil daily to support skin elasticity and keep the nail plate from drying out and becoming prone to chipping.

Protect the Skin Barrier: Treat your hands with the same care you give your face. Use moisture-rich hand creams formulated with ceramides or hyaluronic acid, and always apply broad-spectrum sunscreen to the backs of your hands to protect against UV damage and premature aging.

Change Your Removal Technique: Avoid peeling, scraping, or forcing off gel polish, which can strip keratin layers from the surface of your nail.

Try a Strengthening Treatment: Add repair treatments to your manicure routine, such as keratin strengtheners or at-home red-light therapy devices, for stronger, shinier nails.

#6960. How to Turn Your Shower Into a Spa-Worthy Ritual

This may be the ultimate way to get a break from the holiday rush

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Between gift shopping, family gatherings, and holiday parties, the season can quickly fill every moment of your schedule. An everything shower offers a chance to slow down and indulge in a little self-care, transforming an everyday rinse into a more immersive beauty ritual. Think of it as a moment to reconnect with yourself, recharge, and savor a little calm amid the holiday rush.

The right combination of sensorial fragrances and thoughtfully formulated products from Ulta Beauty can elevate an everything shower into a spa-worthy ritual. From skin-nourishing essentials to indulgent finishing touches, these picks will help you create a routine that feels uniquely your own.

An exfoliating body scrub helps buff away dead skin cells, leaving skin feeling smoother and softer. Tree Hut’s sugar-based formulas pair gentle exfoliation with shea butter and hydrating oils, all wrapped in an inviting fragrance. For a little variety, the Tree Hut Very Vanilla Trio Set features three vanilla-inspired scents: Golden Vanilla, Vanilla, and Tahitian Vanilla.

Fenty Beauty’s Big Dripp’r Body Wash has a rich, velvety texture that lathers into a soft foam, while glycerin helps hydrate skin. Its Lil Miss Wonderful fragrance blends floral and gourmand notes, including candied apple and peony. Find it in the Fenty Skin Body The Driplets Mini Body Wash, Milk + Mist Set, which also includes the matching Butta Drop Hydrating Body Milk and Allover Body Mist for a coordinated scent experience.

After cleansing, a body serum adds another layer of care to your routine. The Uni 24-Hour Hydrating Body Serum is especially worth considering when winter weather leaves skin feeling rough or dry. Its lightweight formula absorbs quickly, with aloe vera and bladderwrack seaweed to help hydrate, while Australian caviar lime and kakadu plum offer exfoliating and antioxidant benefits.

Once you apply your serum, add body oil to help lock in moisture. The Osea Undaria Uplift Trio includes the brand’s bestselling Undaria Algae Body Oil, which has a velvety feel and absorbs quickly without leaving a greasy residue. Formulated with antioxidant-rich Undaria seaweed and a blend of passion fruit, açai, babassu, and rice bran oils, it helps soften skin and replenish moisture. The fresh, citrusy fragrance, with notes of grapefruit, lime, mandarin, and cypress, adds a sensory finishing touch. The set also includes a matching body wash and mood oil, so you can carry the scent through your routine.

Complete your ritual with a final touch of fragrance. The Sol de Janeiro Cheirosa Anywhere Jelly Perfume Balm & Mist Gift Set pairs perfume mists with solid balms designed to last up to 10 hours. Choose Cheirosa 62, a warm gourmand blend of pistachio, almond, and vanilla, or Cheirosa 68, a brighter, more floral composition featuring pink dragonfruit, jasmine, and sun musk. Either way, you’ll carry a little of that just-stepped-out-of-the-spa feeling with you long after your shower.

#6955. All the Stars at the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards

From Hailey Bieber to Elle Fanning to Pamela Anderson

Every fall, the Council of Fashion Designers of America (CFDA) brings together designers, editors, and the biggest names in entertainment to celebrate another year of American fashion. At a dinner held at New York City’s storied Natural History Museum, underneath a 94-foot-long model of a blue whale, new faces and tenured creatives alike are honored for their contributions to the industry—with awards such as the Emerging Designer of the Year and the American Accessory Designer of the Year handed out throughout the evening.

This year, the event was hosted by Keke Palmer, with presenters including the likes of Demi Moore, Naomi Osaka, Lupita Nyong’o, Matt Damon, and Cole Escola. Ralph Lauren took home the American Menswear Designer of the Year award (for the third time!), Rachel Scott of Diotima was awarded American Womenswear Designer of the Year (for the second time), and Ana Khouri was recognized with the aforementioned accessory designer accolade. Colleen Allen won the coveted Emerging Designer trophy, in a stacked lineup of nominees that included Lii’s Zane Li and Emily Dawn Long, among others.

In addition to the winners announced last night, several powerhouses were also spotlighted. Anthony Vaccarello, who recently announced his departure from Saint Laurent after a decade as creative director, was recognized as the International Designer of the Year, joined by YSL muses Hailey Bieber, Zoë Kravitz, Rosé, and Anja Rubik. Tory Burch received the Positive Change Award, Linda Fargo accepted the Founder’s Award in honor of Eleanor Lambert, and Gap Inc., under the direction of Zac Posen, took home the Innovation Award.

The Geoffrey Beene Lifetime Achievement Award went to photographers Inez van Lamsweerde and Vinoodh Matadin, Eva Chen accepted the Media Award in honor of Eugenia Sheppard, and the Isabel Toledo Board of Directors’ Tribute was awarded to Susan Cianciolo. Lastly, the Fashion Icon Award—previously presented to everyone from A$AP Rocky to Zendaya to Naomi Campbell—recognized architect Peter Marino.

Ahead, see all of the guests at the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards.

#6952. This California Cottage Puts a Charming Spin on the "Summer Camp Nostalgia" Aesthetic

Nostalgia rules in this 1930s Northern California home.

When Corine Maggio and her husband bought their 1930s cottage in Mill Valley, California, it was meant to be their starter home. Coming in at about 1,500 square feet, with an awkwardly laid-out kitchen, living room, and entryway, it didn’t seem to be an ideal place to raise their two children—that was, until they realized how rare it was to have a view, a yard, and walkability. Before they knew it, they were falling in love with their home—and all the potential it held.

“The house has an energy you can’t just recreate,” Maggio explains. With three bedrooms and two bathrooms, there was technically enough room for their family of four, and the architectural bones could easily meld with the relaxed, “summer camp in Maine” aesthetic Maggio chases in every project. “We needed to get smart about space planning and bring back a significant amount of character that had been stripped away,” she adds. In other words, the layout had to be revamped, and nearly every wall needed a dose of paneling, paint, or wallpaper—or preferably, some combination of the three.

The kitchen was first on the chopping block. Sandwiched between the laundry room and the dining room, the space felt cut off from the rest of the house in a way that wasn’t conducive to their family-focused, indoor-outdoor lifestyle. Maggio’s solution was to remove the laundry room wall, giving the kitchen access to the backyard, and finish the space with a painted beadboard ceiling to lend it a rustic, timeworn feel.

Mixed patterns, layered textures, and natural materials became Maggio’s way of capturing that summer camp nostalgia without creating a space that felt too formal for everyday life. The narrow living room lent itself well to a circular seating arrangement; when paired with a canopy-inspired wallcovering, the layout mirrored the act of sitting around a campfire to tell stories. Throughout, vintage furniture mingles with custom pieces, and personal touches abound, from the butcher block Maggio found on Facebook Marketplace to a chair left behind by a previous owner.

“I want people to come in and feel something familiar,” Maggio says. “I want them to feel comfortable being themselves, knowing nothing in the house is more precious than the people.”

FAST FACTSDesigner: Corine MaggioLocation: Mill Valley, CaliforniaThe Space: A 1930 cottage with three bedrooms and two bathrooms.

Located in the center of the action—and boasting the best views of the water—this multifunctional space was designed as a place for family and guests to gather while Maggio and her husband cook.

After reviewing more than 30 wallpaper samples, Maggio finally selected this woodland scene by Bunny Williams Home, her least favorite of the bunch until she held it up in the space and saw the transformation it would make.

To visually lighten the galley kitchen, upper cabinets flanking the Ilve range were fitted with glass, breaking up the run of solid cabinetry and creating a more open feel. The butcher-block island is from the 1940s and weighs over 400 pounds.

Originally, there wasn’t a proper mudroom, only a three-walled entry open to the elements. Maggio enclosed it, painting the new beadboard-covered walls in Swans Mill Cream by Benjamin Moore, a cheery yellow that glows in the sunlight.

Maggio’s home office is a detached Tuff Shed in the backyard, to which she added paneling and Soane Britain wallpaper for an English garden vibe. Pinned-up magazine pages on the wall serve as both inspiration and art.

Maggio’s daughter, June, “personifies joy,” so she wanted her retreat to promote imagination and fun. Filled with vintage pieces and clad in a Pierre Frey wallpaper, it feels nostalgic and youthful.

To accentuate the room’s vaulted ceiling, Maggio switched from vertical nickel groove panels to horizontal ones, carrying them up to the room’s peak.

In a controversial move, the designer reduced the room’s square footage and opted for one sink instead of two. “We benefit significantly more from the counter space and storage,” she says. A vintage dresser gets new life as a vanity.

When Corine Maggio started her eponymous design firm, she knew she wanted to create homes for her clients that felt warm, character-rich, and deeply personal. Her goal isn’t to simply design spaces that look beautiful and collected, but to improve how people feel in their daily lives.

#6942. Pamela Anderson, 59, Pairs a Classic Red Manicure With a “Bombshell Bob”

The actress makes a case for minimal makeup and statement curls on the red carpet

In 2023, Pamela Anderson famously vowed to wear less makeup on red carpets. Since then, the actress has attended countless events makeup-free—but she has never left her hair behind. In fact, Anderson, known for her iconic long blond hair, makes her hairstyle the centerpiece, as she did last night at the 2026 Council of Fashion Designers of America (CFDA) Fashion Awards.

Anderson walked the CFDA Fashion Awards red carpet in her typical minimalist makeup look, but with “bombshell curls” inspired by old Hollywood. In a black halter dress with feather detailing, accessorized with a just-bitten berry lip, Anderson wore a coiffed bob with old Hollywood pin curls. Because the curls fell away from her face, they showcased her glowing, fresh skin.

Anderson’s retro hair wasn’t the only statement look last night. She wore fall’s boldest nail trend—a bright, cherry-red almond manicure—contrasting with her satin black-and-white polka-dot clutch. With no shortage of buzzy new nail trends swirling around the internet, like “chili pepper nails” and “vanilla nails,” Anderson proves that sometimes the best shade for your manicure is classic red.

Lately, Anderson has given a master class in the many ways to style bobs. Just a few weeks ago, while at the 2026 Academy Museum Gala in Los Angeles, Anderson wore a head full of curls with subtle bangs and lots of volume.

In an interview with Anderson last year, she shared her hair philosophy—why she never sticks to one style. “I think people associate me with big blond hair, but if I look at my whole life, I’ve always been one to change up the way that I look,” Anderson told Bazaar. “Your hair is another way to express yourself, and I’ve never been shy.”

She continued: “I guess I’m genetically blessed, but I’ve always had very thick, healthy hair. I do like to cut it and to style it differently. It’s freeing. I am a big lover of change; that’s what keeps us young and excited.”

#6938. “Smoky Steel” Is the Simple Color Technique That Instantly Adds Shine and Dimension to Gray Hair

Lustrous, modern, and endlessly sophisticated

The notion that gray hair lacks versatility couldn't be further from the truth: from “silver framing” to “frosted hair,” innovative color trends keep proving how chic it can be. The latest to join the roster? “Smoky steel.”

With its cool, dark undertones and soft, silvery dimension, this modern take on gray hair makes a compelling case for embracing the shade at every age. Seen to great effect on Andie MacDowell and, more recently, Olivia Colman, smoky steel strikes the perfect balance between sophisticated and modern—whether worn in long cascading curls, cut into a sleek bob, or styled in a polished topknot.

The hue's depth makes it even more appealing. Rather than a classic uniform gray, smoky steel adds density by combining dark, ashy tones with cool silver, creating dimension in the light. “By adding subtle beige, taupe, or mink warmth into gray, the color looks more luminous against the skin as it creates a healthy, diffused glow,” Adam Russell, creative master colorist at Josh Wood, previously told Harper’s Bazaar. “Warm grays also blend beautifully with naturally graying hair, so the growing-out process is far more seamless.”

This is especially true for smoky steel, which sits somewhere between classic silver and ashy brunette. Its softer, multitonal finish blends more easily with natural grays, creating a result that feels both effortless and polished. “These shades balance warm and cool pigments, keeping the color looking chic rather than yellow or brassy,” says Russell.

A skilled colorist can also soften the contrast between sharper roots and lighter lengths, adding shine and dimension with strategic highlights. However, allowing silver strands to emerge without coloring can be just as striking.

Ultimately, smoky steel reminds us that gray hair doesn’t mean compromising on a dynamic, multidimensional look. If you've ever had a reason to reconsider your next hair appointment, this might be it.

Fans started suspecting that something was going on between Charles and Alexandra in early 2023, after the two were spotted together at Paris Fashion Week. At the time, Alexandra's social media was reportedly private and Charles didn't address the speculation until a few months later.

Charles eventually put all the rumors to rest, confirming that he was dating someone in an interview in May 2023. Though he didn’t name Alexandra at the time, the two made their debut as a couple a few months later.

Surprise! Charles and Alexandra tied the knot in Monaco with a civil ceremony at Villa La Vigie (and of course, Leo was part of the celebration).

The couple sparked pregnancy rumors when they showed up at the Hungarian Grand Prix with Leo in tow...as well as a possible baby bump.

#6936. 15 Paint Colors HGTV Stars Swear By for a Home That Looks Expensive

From sea blue to crisp white, these designer favorites are timeless.

Everyone knows that a quick coat of paint is the fastest way to transform a room. It’s a favorite trick among professional designers and DIYers alike, but choosing the perfect shade isn't always easy. With hundreds of colors to choose from, finding “the one” can quickly become overwhelming. If you’re feeling a little stuck, don’t fret: HGTV’s biggest stars make these tough decisions every day.

Whether they’re renovating an outdated home or putting the finishing touches on a dream build, HGTV personalities are constantly selecting paint colors that make a space feel fresh, spacious, inviting, and beautifully designed. Over the years, they’ve crowned a select few as go-to favorites that are foolproof and versatile enough to work in almost any room. When every renovation comes with deadlines and budgets, dependable paint colors become invaluable.

To help narrow your search, we asked some of our favorite HGTV stars to reveal the paint colors they reach for most often. Their picks include everything from versatile neutrals to powdery pastels that work across a variety of homes and aesthetics. Additional copy by Alyssa Khan.

“We love muted colors because they’re playful, but not over-the-top. Behr Mocha Ice is a perfect example of that balance—it brings the personality you need to take a space from basic and bland to warm and interesting. Try it in a bedroom, dining room, or anywhere that’s missing that ‘wow’ factor.”—Drew Scott, Property Brothers

“This beautiful blue is timeless and versatile. It’s calming, but not boring. Even though Behr's Dragonfly makes a statement, it’s still neutral enough for many spaces, whether it’s on every wall in a bedroom or used on a specific feature like kitchen cabinets.”—Jonathan Scott, Property Brothers

“This never-boring black paint color is dramatic and impactful yet very versatile. Pair with white for a timeless aesthetic. Use it to create a dramatic accent wall or outside for your exterior (like we did) or trim for maximum curb appeal.”—Tarek El Moussa, The Flip Off

“My favorite paint color is Tricorn Black by Sherwin-Williams. It’s bold, clean, and makes every detail pop. Whether it’s a door, a wall, or literally anything else, this black brings that high-end look every single time. Never fails.”—Cole DeBoer, Down Home Fab

“I am currently loving greige, the perfect blend of gray and beige. It’s warm, soft, and timeless—a great option for homeowners who want both cozy and modern. Benjamin Moore's Revere Pewter is a classic and rich greige with warmth. Great for open-concept homes.”—Heather Rae El Moussa, The Flip Off

“What I love most about it is its confidence. Studio Green by Farrow & Ball doesn’t whisper: It commands. It’s deep, moody, and unapologetically rich, the kind of color that instantly elevates a space and gives it a point of view. In daylight, it reads as a classic, almost architectural green, grounded and refined. But at night? It transforms into something velvety and dramatic, wrapping the room in a quiet luxury that feels both intimate and powerful.”—Egypt Sherrod, Married to Real Estate andProperty Virgins

“Benjamin Moore Misty Air OC-44 is my constant when I need a bright neutral. It is a broken-in cream that’s the perfect backdrop for colorful art and upholstery.”—Erin Napier, Home Town

““My current favorite paint color is Black Fox by Sherwin-Williams. It’s the perfect mix of sophisticated and moody, with just enough warmth to make any space feel grounded and timeless. It pairs beautifully with so many other tones and textures. I love it in an office!”—Chelsea DeBoer, Down Home Fab

Magnolia Home by Joanna Gaines Juniper Tree Crafted by KILZ

“Seed & Supply was one of the first additions we made to the Silos back in 2015. Before it served as our garden shop on the grounds, it was used as an additional retail space at the original Magnolia shop, which we lovingly refer to as The Little Shop on Bosque. This year, the team had the idea to evolve the building yet again, but this time to serve as a creative workshop space for our guests. I immediately loved the idea. It has always been my hope that our guests’ experience at the Silos would inspire creativity and connection.

The whole interior of the workshop is refreshed with one color, Juniper Tree Trim, Door, and Cabinetry Paint in satin finish. I love how these nature-forward hues turned out—they feel fresh yet familiar, bold yet comforting.”—Joanna Gaines, Fixer Upper

““White Flour is the perfect soft white backdrop for every style. It accentuates simplicity or maximalist energy. The warm hue is anything but basic.”—Taniya Nayak, Battle on the Beach

“I love Farrow & Ball's Drop Cloth for walls, or for trim and cabinetry with a more true white on the wall. It is a timeless neutral that looks beautiful, particularly in historic homes. This warm neutral evokes calm tranquility without being sterile. It is the perfect first layer for any space: It sets a foundation of simplicity that can easily be layered upon with color on cabinetry, trim detail, furniture, art, and décor.”—Jenny Marrs, Fixer to Fabulous

“I keep going back to Behr White. It’s porcelain-like and a classic white without being too stark or jarring. It also has a very slight undertone of blue, which I prefer over yellow or pink undertones, as a slight hint of blue preserves more of a pure white tone.”—Galey Alix, Home in a Heartbeat With Galey Alix

“My current favorite color is Benjamin Moore Windsor Green. I just painted my bedroom this color! The walls, the doors, the windows, and the trim. It's just so deep and lovely. In her blog for the Laurel Mercantile, the Home Town Takeover co-host crowned Dover White her all-time favorite hue.

#6934. The 11 Best Fragrances Released This October

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

The new smoky, spicy scents you need to know about.

I love fragrance. I love fall. Put the two together, and I’m in olfactory heaven. I’ve been excited to put this roundup together since the first new spooky scents started hitting shelves early last month—as we get into November, it’s evident that October will be a tough one to follow up.

There’s plenty of spice and smoke in the air, plus rich gourmands that feel tailor-made for colder weather. There’s even a new fragrance collection in partnership with Robert Eggers’s latest gothic horror film, for anyone who’s ever wanted to smell like they could turn into a werewolf at any moment—or, well, just want to smell like they’ve been at a bonfire. Ready to find your next fall scent? Read on for 11 exciting new fragrances you can get now.

If you’re looking for a new go-to fragrance for fall and winter, let it be Commodity Spice. Like a hot cup of chai, this expressive fragrance is warm and smooth with a rich cocktail of spices including saffron, nutmeg, clove, cardamom, and cinnamon. If I close my eyes, I feel like I just stepped into a cozy little tea and spices shop, and I want to wear that feeling like a sweater.

When Fulton & Roark released Cathedral of Ruins, it quickly became one of our favorite cold-weather smoky scents. Not only does it have a super unique aroma of smoldering frankincense, black pepper, rose, and woods, but it has a heartbreakingly beautiful origin story—the brand’s founders were inspired to create the scent after visiting the abandoned City Methodist Church in Gary, Indiana. The fragrance is an homage to the religious rituals that once took place in that church.

Think of a buttery croissant; a butterscotch candy; a cinnamon roll with sweet, creamy frosting. Those are the vibes of Gap’s newest fragrance. A late addition to the newly (re)launched collection of EDPs, it joins the group as the first gourmand—just in time for fall. Oh, and if you like how Bliss smells, you can complete the collection with the scented hand cream and body lotion.

I was always under the impression that fruity fragrances and woody ones had very little to do with each other and that, for the most part, they should stay separate. That’s until I smelled Maison d’Etto’s Pinehurst. Here, fruity notes of rambutan, blood orange, and ripe pineapple provide a beautiful overlay to base notes of leather, oud, and other woods. It’s a radiant scent that works year-round, day and night.

David Moltz (the “D.S.” of D.S. & Durga) was inspired to create this Resurrected Corpse Flower fragrance after visiting the Brooklyn Botanical Garden during the height of a rare blooming of a corpse flower. What he smelled was, in his words, “like a baby diaper pail in a rotten fish tank full of rubber lily bulbs & olives.” But then he got to thinking, what if that scent could be cleaned of all its rotten odors and reborn into something beautiful? Thus, Resurrected Corpse Flower was born. With green olive, lily, tuberose, freesia, and deep bog cedar, it’s a truly special scent that’s at once floral and swampy.

Because this fragrance is so unlike anything I’ve ever smelled before, it might be a bit polarizing; I’m sure some of you will love it, and others might prefer one of D.S. & Durga’s other unique fragrances. But after leaving this scent on my skin for a bit, I really started to appreciate it.

In contrast to some of the deep, rich gourmands on this list, Lumira’s Soléor offers a lighter, sun-soaked gourmand option. Instead of creamy sweetness, it’s more of a soft, sensual sweetness that isn’t too heavy or overpowering. Smooth amber and candied fruit up top give it a bit of a citrusy kick, which fades into caramel, vanilla, and woods. It works for fall and winter just as well as it does for spring and summer.

If you do want something rich and creamy, Phlur’s Heavy Cream is the way to go. It’s an ambery gourmand with notes of vanilla mousse, white orchid, and creamy musk, which are balanced out by hints of sparkling lemon and orange flower, adding a little bit of lightness to the otherwise heavy fragrance. And, of course, with a name like Heavy Cream, there has to be a coordinating whipped body butter for the full experience.

How do you make decadence refined? You create a rich, vanilla gourmand and add the sophisticated warmth of Peru balsam. It’s definitely a sweet scent, and the combination of Cristal vanilla, bourbon vanilla, tonka bean, and black sugar creates the aroma of a classic crème brûlée. But once you get past the creamy sweetness of it all, you’re left with a sensual, balsamic scent that lingers.

I didn’t know a fragrance could smell so much like Halloween, but Heretic teamed up with Robert Eggers’ new movie Werwulf and did just that. At first, you get spice and smoke from cardamom, smoke, and leather; it then moves into something woody, earthy, and animalic with fir balsam, labdanum, patchouli, moss, and damp earth. The “suggestion of wet fur” is what really makes this scent stand out—and what gives it that spooky edge. Wear with your scariest Halloween costume.

In a departure from the mostly spicy, rich, gourmand scents that hit shelves this October, Drake’s Better World Fragrance House partnered with October’s Very Own for a fresh, citrusy fragrance that gives the impression of a refreshing white wine sangria with notes of passion fruit, rose, white cedarwood, and roasted tonka bean. But in its spherical burgundy glass bottle, it’ll blend in perfectly with your other fall and winter scents.

Roll-on fragrance oils are all the rage right now, and new fragrance house Gamine has already come out with its own collection of fragrance oils called Microdose. With six parfum oils, you can wear each individually or combine two or three for your own unique scent. My favorite of the bunch is DMT, a metallic fragrance built on notes of burnt latex, aerated foam, and hot steel. I wasn’t sure I would like it based on the description—and the fact that I’d never heard of a metallic scent before—but I’m actually obsessed with it.

#6932. Teyana Taylor Does Tuxedo Dressing in a White Corset and Silky Oversize Pants

She meant business at the NYC premiere of All’s Fair season two

#6930. 20 Lucky Plants That Bring Good Fortune and Major Good Vibes to Your Home

Houseplants are more than just mere accessories. Plants are beacons of vibrant, flowing, living energy, symbolizing many sentiments, from “Get Well” to “Congrats on the new gig.” Like colors, crystals, and trinkets, it’s always fun to explore the meanings behind your greenery to add an extra layer of intention to your windowsill, flower beds, or next potted gift. If you're looking to usher in more good vibes (and who isnt?), adding lucky plants to your home might be just what you need.

Feng shui principles have long held that plants bring good energy to your home. Simply appreciating their leaves and branches instantly adds vivaciousness to any room. That said, wealth, peace, and health aren’t the only things that good luck plants provide. Research shows plants can boost mood, improve sleep, and help us feel happier, even less isolated. When you factor those benefits into the equation, it'd be silly not to snag yourself a plant or two, right?

Before you rush to the nearest nursery, you’ll need to account for the conditions plants need to thrive—a dying plant won’t bring you an abundance of much. As a general rule of thumb, most indoor houseplants (especially tropicals) prefer to bask in moderate to bright light, easily achieved by placing them near a window that gets lots of soft morning light. If your rooms are dark or windowless, you can always opt for a grow light. Watering is also important, but hitting the proper cadence is essential. In fact, more plants die from overwatering than from under. To avoid drowning your plant, poke your finger in the soil before giving it a drink; if soil clings to your skin, it’s probably moist enough. Wait a few more days, then recheck.

Ahead, you'll find 20 good luck plants that thrive in a variety of indoor settings. From glam orchids to classic lucky bamboo (of course!), these greens will bring good juju to your home for years to come.

Despite its name, this good-luck plant isn't true bamboo. It's a type of dracaena that has the lower foliage stripped off to resemble bamboo. It's incredibly easy to grow and care for, making it ideal for plant parents of all experience levels. Lucky bamboo does best in medium-to-bright indirect light but will tolerate low light. If yours is growing in a container of water, change the water weekly—if planted in soil, keep it slightly moist.

Beloved for their durable nature and eye-catching blooms, orchids have a glamorous air about them—and a reputation for symbolizing fertility and abundance. Though they may look high-maintenance, orchids are relatively easy to care for; place them in a spot that gets at least six hours of indirect light daily and "water" them by placing three ice cubes on the soil once a week.

Oxalis, or lucky shamrock plant, is associated with St. Patrick's Day and is said to bring good luck to the home. With three-lobed green or burgundy foliage and delicate flowers that bloom for weeks, these seasonal favorites are long-lived and low maintenance. Give this good-luck plant bright, indirect light and allow it to dry out slightly between waterings.

Vibrant, funky, and known to bring good fortune, bromeliad plants are the answer to any plant lover looking to add a little color to their collection. A close cousin to the pineapple, these bright tropicals come in a variety of beautiful shades (think bright pink, zesty yellow, or fiery red). Place the plant somewhere with plenty of light and water it every one to two weeks in warmer months, reducing your cadence to every two to three weeks in colder months.

The glossy, coin-shaped leaves of a pilea plant (aka Chinese money plant) are purported to bring wealth and luck. It's also super easy to propagate and share with loved ones, which lends a sense of prosperity to the giver. Give it bright, indirect light, and water when the soil feels mostly dry.

Though it may be known by rather unpleasant names (it also goes by mother-in-law's tongue), snake plants make a beautiful addition to any home, thanks to their graphic variegated leaves and vibrant green hue. These good-luck plants are known to invite fortune, good health, and creativity into the home and are incredibly easy to care for, thriving in a variety of light settings and only requiring watering every two to three weeks.

Also called Pachira aquatica, the money tree is said to bring its owner wealth and is often given to new businesses to wish them good fortune. Some even consider money trees to be the luckiest houseplants of all. This sturdy plant prefers medium to bright indirect light, but will tolerate low light levels just fine too. Rotate the pot once a week so that the branches will grow evenly, and water when the top inch or two of soil feels dry.

It's no surprise that this elegant plant is purported to bring harmony to your home. Peace lilies have glossy green leaves and pretty spoon-shaped flowers. Give this good-luck plant bright, indirect light, and keep the soil lightly moist. Pet parents: It's best to avoid this variety, since it's especially toxic to cats.

A symbol of longevity and good luck, chrysanthemums are a beautiful plant to enjoy indoors and out. Also called mums, these flowers are perennial if you plant them in the spring in your garden and can be enjoyed as gifts any time of year (they'll bloom indoors for several weeks). Just water them when the soil feels slightly dry on top.

Jade plants are said to bring wealth and longevity and can live for decades with the proper care. Resembling tiny trees with plump, glossy leaves, they prefer bright, indirect light over direct rays, which can scorch their delicate leaves. Because they're succulents, you should only water this good-luck plant when the top inch or two of soil feels dry—overwatering will cause them to turn to mush.

The gorgeous peony is a symbol of wealth and rank. This stunning perennial can live for decades when planted in the proper USDA growing zone. Choose a spot that receives direct sunlight for six or more hours a day and water the plant weekly to get it established; after that, peonies are drought tolerant.

A symbol of prosperity since ancient times, citrus trees—Meyer lemon, kumquat, take your pick—make a wonderful indoor or patio plant. Give these trees direct sun for six or more hours per day, or use a grow light—without ample light, they're unlikely to produce fruit. Water when the soil feels slightly dry on top.

Lavender flowers are said to bring serenity, and studies show that they actually live up to the hype, thanks to powerful phytochemicals that can be mood boosters for both people and pets. Lavender can be fussy to grow indoors, but it's a cinch to grow outdoors. Just give it full sun and make sure it's in a well-draining spot (or a container, if you have clay soil). Water only when very dry.

With a single, heart-shaped leaf, this adorable good-luck plant is a wonderful gift to show affection or care—in fact, hoya is known to represent true love and harmony. Give it bright, indirect light, and water when the top of the soil feels dry to the touch, usually every week or two.

The scent of a rosemary plant can calm the body and energize the mind, and it was once thought to bring good luck to travelers. This culinary herb is a tender perennial, which means it can be planted in the garden in warm climates. Give it full sun, and water it only sparingly; this native Mediterranean plant prefers to be on the dry side. Indoors, it needs a sunny windowsill with direct sunlight or a grow light.

The bold leaves of the zebra plant bring good fortune, wealth, and steady progress. Feng Shui traditions believed that the upward-growing leaves and resilient roots acted as a protective charm to ward off negative energy and balance indoor spaces.

Like its cousin, the common snake plant, the copper-toned variety is also known to represent resilience, protection, and good fortune, just with a shinier hue.

Feng Shui philosophy believes the heart-shaped leaves of the philodendron bring love, balance, and mental calmness. The plant is also a known air purifier that loves low-light environments. It must be the ambiance.

There’s a reason the olive tree is recognized as a universal symbol of peace. In Greek mythology, the Goddess Athena gifted the tree to the people of Athens, ultimately helping them win the city. Today, the plant is rumored to bring that same sense of peace, safety, and balance into homes.

Known for its fresh scent and medicinal properties, eucalyptus is thought to bring spa-like serenity and elevated wellness into your home.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6929. Just When I Thought We Were Done with Alina Habba, She’s Back in the News for a Doozy of a Reason

Why is it that everyone who’s ever come in close personal contact with Donald Trump turns out to be a shady grifter?

One of the late, unlamented Whitey Bulger’s most notorious crimes was the seizure of a South Boston liquor store. From CBS News:

(Rakes never saw full restitution. He was poisoned in 2013, in what prosecutors said was an unrelated episode. A 72-year old man named William Camuti, who owed Rakes a substantial amount of money, was convicted of lacing Rakes’s coffee with cyanide and sentenced to life.)

The technique never changes. In its most recent iteration, it comes not at a Southie liquor store, but from Mar-a-Lago, complete with rubbery shrimp. Let us explain.

#6928. When Is ‘Rivals’ Season 2, Part 2 Out? Full Release Schedule and How to Watch

Rutshire’s messiest residents are back for more drama.

The shoulder pads are back, the champagne is flowing, and the drama in Rutshire is showing absolutely no signs of slowing down.

If you've been counting down the days until Rivals returns, you're certainly not alone. Disney+'s gloriously scandalous adaptation of Jilly Cooper's best-selling novel has given us everything we could possibly want from a period drama: outrageous affairs, ruthless power plays, spectacular ‘80s fashion, and a cast of characters who are almost incapable of behaving themselves.

But rather than dropping every episode at once, Disney+ has split season 2 into two parts, leaving viewers with a wait between installments.

So, when exactly does Rivals season 2, part 2 arrive, and how many episodes are left? Here's everything you need to know.

The wait is almost over! Rivals season 2 part 2 will premiere on Disney+ on Wednesday, November 11, 2026, with three brand-new episodes landing at once.

That's right, we'll be getting a triple helping of Rutshire scandal before the series switches to weekly releases for its final three episodes.

The second season consists of 12 episodes in total, with the first six having aired earlier this year.

And following THAT devastating mid-season cliffhanger, there's plenty of unfinished business to get through.

Disney+ will release the remaining six episodes across four Wednesdays, with the season finale arriving in December.

So, you'll have three episodes to binge on November 11, followed by one new episode every Wednesday until the finale.

*Warning: Spoilers for season 2, part 1 ahead!

When we last visited Rutshire, things had taken a devastating turn.

Lady Monica Baddingham (Claire Rushbrook) was killed in a car accident after discovering that her husband Tony (David Tennant) had been having an affair with Maud O'Hara (Victoria Smurfit).

And with Tony now grieving, the battle between Corinium Television and rival company Venturer looks set to become even more ruthless.

Meanwhile, Rupert Campbell-Black (Alex Hassell) is facing some difficult decisions of his own, particularly when it comes to his relationship with Taggie O'Hara (Bella Maclean).

According to Disney+, the second half of the season will see affairs unravel, alliances fracture, and long-buried secrets threaten to come to light.

Expect plenty more ’80s excess, from lavish parties and the Hampshire Hunt Ball to Bonfire Night celebrations, and a particularly chaotic Christmas.

There are also some new faces joining the drama, including Rachael Stirling as Monica's younger sister Araminta Pemberton, Rupert Evans as headmaster David Hawkley, and Santiago Cabrera as Argentinian polo player Alejandro Mendoza.

In other words, Rutshire isn't about to get any quieter.

There's good news for anyone already dreading the season 2 finale.

Disney+ has officially renewed Rivals for a third season, with another 12 episodes on the way.

While there's no confirmed release date just yet, at least we know the scandals, romance, and spectacularly questionable decisions aren't ending with season 2.

Both seasons of Rivals are available on Disney+ and Hulu.

All eight episodes of season 1 and the first six episodes of season 1 are available to stream now, meaning there's still time to catch up before part 2 arrives.

And if you've somehow managed to avoid the drama until now, consider this your invitation to enter the wonderfully chaotic world of Rutshire.

#6923. Hailey Bieber Is Pledging $5 Million to Women’s Health Research After Her Own Postpartum Struggles

The Rhode founder secretly battled premenstrual dysphoric disorder after giving birth to baby Jack.

Hailey Bieber is putting her money where her mouth is. The Rhode founder is pledging $5 million toward women’s health research inspired by her own tumultuous postpartum journey.

The cause hits close to home for Hailey, who revealed she secretly struggled with premenstrual dysphoric disorder, or PMDD, after giving birth to Jack Blues in August 2024. According to Johns Hopkins Medicine, PMDD is “a much more severe form of premenstrual syndrome,” or PMS.

“After I had my son, I struggled with that a bit, and there’s just not a lot of conversation or research around it,” she told WWD, adding that she’s committed to raising awareness for women’s health issues like PMDD. The condition affects 1.6 percent of women and girls globally, according to research by the University of Oxford.

“Conversation, for me personally, makes me feel less alone,” she continued. “When we can identify with each other and have conversations relating to each other and talking about the things we’re dealing with and struggling with, I do think it makes it feel like less of a lonely journey.”

Hailey also shared she “feels really drawn to women’s health” because she and her loved ones have had different diagnoses that affected their lifestyles.

“I have a sister who struggles with PCOS. I have lots of friends that struggle with it, with endometriosis, with a lot of different, very specific women’s health issues,” she explained. “As a woman, I feel frustrated by the lack of research, the lack of awareness, the lack of certain conversation around it.”

The beauty mogul’s generous pledge will reportedly “support medical research, education, and care in historically underfunded areas, including pregnancy and postpartum health and autoimmune conditions.”

According to WWD, proposals have already been submitted by research institutions, and grant recipients will be announced sometime next year.
