#6919. “Who Knew Zorro Was So Important?” Catherine Zeta-Jones Looks Back at Her Life in Movies in Bazaar’s “Building Character” Series

The Welsh actress reveals the secrets of some of her biggest roles, from Cabaret to Wednesday

Since the 1990s, Catherine Zeta-Jones has played a bevvy of strong women on screen who hold their own space—weapons and all—against powerful men. For Harper’s Bazaar’s latest installment of the video series “Building Character,” the Welsh actress looks back at her storied career, beginning with her first-ever television role as Mariette in The Darling Buds of May, a British comedy-drama that ran on the ITV Network in England from 1991 to 1993.

It was another small project she embarked upon after moving to America, a Titanic TV series (no, really) that aired on CBS, that would go on to change her life. Apparently Steven Spielberg caught one of those early episodes “on a Sunday night, as if he had nothing else to do in his life,” as she explains it, and was so taken with the young actress that he made sure that Martin Campbell, who directed 1998’s The Mask of Zorro, would cast her in his film. Within one week, she was taking riding and fencing lessons in preparation for the movie, which she starred in opposite Antonio Banderas. As if that wasn’t enough, “My husband [Michael Douglas] saw me in it and fell in love with me, so…” she says, laughing. “Who knew Zorro was so important?”

In the full video above, she talks about starring in Zorro and Chicago, finally feeling cool during the filming of Ocean’s Eleven, and how her mother reacted after she was cast in Entrapment.

#6914. A Look Inside the 2026 Dia Art Foundation Fall Gala

Attendees included Sofia Coppola, Marion Cotillard, and Matthieu Blazy

Last night marked a busy evening in the world of fashion. In New York City, the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards were hosted at the American Museum of Natural History, while in Los Angeles, a party was held to celebrate a new book documenting photographer Richard Avedon’s work in Harper’s Bazaar. But that wasn’t all—even more members of the fashion and art worlds united for the Dia Art Foundation’s annual fall gala in NYC.

The event acted as a celebration of two creatives—artist Roni Horn and British film director Steve McQueen—as well as a fundraiser for the arts. Guests gathered at the Dia Chelsea museum before migrating to a cocktail party and dinner where the honorees were toasted.

Some of the starry names in attendance included director Sofia Coppola and Oscar-winning actor Marion Cotillard. This is the first year that the gala has been sponsored by Chanel, after the French fashion house announced its partnership with the non-profit organization back in May. So, this naturally meant that Chanel’s current creative director, Matthieu Blazy, was also present (and he has been teaming up with the organization for years, even since he was at the helm of Bottega Veneta). In 2027, Chanel will sponsor the Dia Beacon gallery’s annual Spring Benefit luncheon, too.

Remarks were shared by the chairman of the Dia Foundation’s board of trustees, Nathalie de Gunzburg; the Dia director, Jessica Morgan; as well as by Matthew Barney, who paid tribute to Horn. Proceeds from the evening will go towards the foundation’s restoration projects, while also contributing to art-related lectures, commissions, educational programs, and more.

#6912. Our Last Chance to Save This Country Begins Today. Let’s Not Waste It.

Don’t even start thinking about the 2028 presidential election. Start thinking about what we’re going to do tomorrow and the day after that.

(Optional Video Accompaniment to This Post)

It is a decade now since the president came down the golden escalator and the country went straight to hell. All we’ve done as citizens of a self-governing democratic republic is squabble over the seating in the handbasket. Until Tuesday. Tuesday was nut-cutting time. Tuesday felt—probably rightly—like the beginning of an extended last chance that the country will have to spend every waking hour pursuing.

Just on Monday, the president closed out his demented road show with a weird address in front of one of his administration’s most conspicuous blunders: the Reflecting Pool. Meanwhile, the Supreme Court, with his carefully manufactured conservative majority, gave the boot to his latest attempt to ratfck the California elections, and the court did so without even a hint of an explanation. Then the president capped a rich, full day by bombing an Iranian island in the Persian Gulf, although I guess he went a little bananas on his little boutique website.

Mama said there’d be days like this. But she never said there’d be so many. And all the rest of them are on us. If I can offer one piece of advice it would be this: Ignore anything written that references the 2028 presidential election. If things break the way the polls say they will, there will be so much concentration required to do what we can to ameliorate the damage this administration has done and is capable of doing over the next two years. Starting the 2028 horse-race coverage this week would be yet another crime against poor, battered journalism. As the late Senator Edward Kennedy told a Democratic convention long ago:

Run through the tape, America. Finish the race.

#6910. Who Pays for ‘Love Is Blind’ Weddings? Let’s Break Down the Cost (and How Much $ Netflix Actually Drops)

It’s that time of the year again! After partaking in the world’s most bizarre version of speed dating possible, the couples of Love Is Blind are ready to get married on camera. The weddings are a chance for everyone to either say “I do” or “I don’t” in front of their friends, family, and thousands of strangers logged into their friend’s ex-boyfriend’s Netflix subscription, and you might be wondering who foots the bill.

Thankfully, the show’s couples do not pay for their own wedding ceremony. Netflix takes care of the cost, and yep, there is a strict budget.

Wedding planner Slomique Hawrylo told Tudum that she has couples fill out a questionnaire about their wedding theme (“traditional” or “boho” for example), their likes and dislikes, and a general vibe. “What I’m doing is looking at what they write on a piece of paper, and getting the feelings and emotions from that,” she explained. “It’s capturing their style, what they want, a certain color.”

And considering there can be multiple a week depending on how many couples are getting married (or at least making it to their ceremony) per season, it’s a lot of work. The team is tasked with hiring local vendors, figuring out food, florals, the cake—the typical things that would go into planning any non-televised wedding (only on a way tighter time crunch).

The show allots a pool of money to plan five weddings on the show, and it’s up to Slomique how that money is allotted. In season 4, Tudum reports that florals cost $50,000 to $60,000, while tents were $120,000.

Any more would stretch the budget too much!

In some cases, the bride and groom will pay for extras that the show is unwilling to cover. Examples? Specific photographers that are outside the budget, specific nail artists, or details like monograms, which are “really, really difficult” for the team in the short time frame.

Love Is Blind creator Chris Coelen told Newsweek, “If there’s anything over and above what we would normally do, then [the couples] are most certainly welcome to do that.”

There was one “I do” on Love Is Blind season 11, and the show clearly went all out! Though...did the relationship last? Spoilers below:

#6909. Louisa Jacobson’s Suited Streetwear Couldn’t Be More Different From Her Gilded Age Character’s Style

From corsets to bustles to floor-length skirts, the womenswear of the Gilded Age was known for many things. But Louisa Jacobson’s frayed raw hems and buckled collars were certainly not in the ladies’ 1880s style guides.

While doing press in New York City for the fourth season of her smash HBO historical drama The Gilded Age, Jacobson showed us a bit of her own street style... and it was a far cry from the looks that her character, Marian Brook, has donned on the very same streets.

Leaning instead into a bit of menswear, rather than the pastel gowns we’ve come to expect from her on-screen alter-ego, the actor experimented with suiting and modernized tailoring, thanks to the help of her stylist, Edward Bowleg III.

Last Monday, Jacobson turned to Mel Usine for a light-gray set from the label’s Spring/Summer 2027 collection. Composed of a frayed, collarless jacket and matching inside-out skirt, the ensemble offered an unfinished, deconstructed look you’d have never seen during the 1883 Metropolitan Opera House opening.

It was layered over a black-trimmed white tank with a skinny red tie wrapped around her waist, and paired with peep-toe heels.

The next day, the actor pushed things even further, wearing a black-on-black monochrome look from Commission’s Spring/Summer 2027 collection. This one was all about the buckle accents, with a zip-up blazer topped with a belted collar and little capri leggings trimmed with the same detail. In between was a pleated skirt, and for shoes, Jacobson wore pointed-toe pumps over calf-high socks.

Later in the day, Jacobson was a proper Calvin Klein girl—seen in Hudson Square wearing a dark-clay satin blazer and matching trousers with a heather-gray jacket over both. Her little nude ballet flats featured a contrasting reflective strap, and she grabbed a shoulder bag before heading out for an afternoon of press.

In an age of constant method dressing, it appears Jacobson is doing things her way.

#6905. Are ‘Love Is Blind’ Weddings Legally Binding? Everything We Know About the Wedding and Divorce Rules

I need to know just how “married” these couples really are....

Welp, another batch of strangers just had elaborate wedding ceremonies on reality TV! Love Is Blind Season 11 dropped its finale, and we found out which couples got married. This year, John and Sarah ended up saying “I do” at the altar, but were these weddings actually legally binding, or did Netflix pull a Let’s Marry Harry and leave the paperwork out of it? About that...

They most certainly are. The couples are married by real officiants, and real paperwork is filed. Meaning, if things don’t work out they have to go through divorce proceedings just like any other couple. As creator Chris Coelen told Bustle in 2020, "If they choose to get married, yes, it's a legally binding marriage."

Former cast member Lauren Speed also told Refinery29 that she and Cameron Hamilton “definitely did” get married, and that was the “part that was so scary for me. This is a real legally binding marriage. This is not for TV. This is our life."

In fact, the legitimacy of the Love Is Blind weddings is one of the ways the show gets spoiled. TMZ dug into marriage records and spoiled several weddings back in 2020, and fans on Reddit have also been known to sleuth.

According to The Tab, the same day as the wedding. The outlet reports that “it’s all done on the same day, often with a judge or ordained officiant present.”

Couples reportedly have to wait until 11 months after their wedding so that the show isn’t spoiled for viewers. As for who foots the bill, The Tab reports that production pays for up to $5,000 per couple for filings and mediation.

#6904. How to Style White Jeans for Fall

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

These outfits prove white denim has no off-season

White jeans are now a year-round staple, no longer relegated to the summer months between Memorial Day and Labor Day. From work-ready looks anchored by tailored denim to relaxed ensembles made for weekend getaways, these white-denim outfits do it all. This fall, take inspiration from a few of our favorite street-style looks. The lineup includes everything from straight-leg and relaxed fits to low-rise baggy silhouettes, in a range of soft hues like off-white and ecru. When paired with capsule wardrobe classics such as a white T-shirt, a brightly colored button-down shirt, or a barn jacket, the results are stylish and equally easy to pull off.

Below, discover 10 outfits with white jeans that prove how cool they can look throughout the fall and winter months.

There’s a reason winter whites are a perennial favorite. Matching white jeans with your outerwear creates a clean, fresh monochromatic look. For a subtle sliver of color, layer an icy blue cardigan underneath, then add classic black pumps, and finish with sculptural gold earrings.

A simple way to wear white jeans during the fall is pairing them with a ladylike jacket with a brown check print. The cropped silhouette offers a flattering counterpoint to relaxed denim, while black accessories—a roomy shoulder bag and ballet flats—lend a refined touch. Consider this an easy fall workwear formula you can wear on repeat.

If you’re a minimalist, try playing with contrasting textures to make your white jeans feel even more seasonally appropriate. Style them with a roomy black suede funnel-neck jacket, pointed-toe boots, and an unexpected accessory, like this elegant tassel bag.

Eterne Fitted Crewneck Short Sleeve T-Shirt

The best fall outfits start with great basics: white straight-leg jeans, a white T-shirt, and a lightweight trench, which are all part of a well-rounded capsule wardrobe. Give these classics a hint of surprise by tying a pop-of-red knit around your shoulders and slipping into mules for an unexpected detail. They’re ideal for peak fall days, when it’s not too hot or too cold and you can get away with a little peep-toe.

Citizens of Humanity Annina Straight-Leg Jeans

A striped long-sleeve T-shirt lends a touch of nautical charm to white jeans. Go for an oversize option in red to play up the relaxed, off-duty vibe. Black shoes—whether boots, loafers, or sleek sneakers—anchor the look, while a leather bucket bag and aviator frames complete the outfit.

Saint Laurent Aviator-Frame Acetate Sunglasses

Barn jackets remain a major trend for fall 2026. Make a case for wearing pastels well into the cooler months with a pale-pink version and white jeans, offset by a black sweater, loafers, and a brown suede bag. Whether for a casual day spent running errands in the city or a weekend away, this combination strikes the perfect balance.

Banana Republic Lightweight Cashmere Crewneck Sweater

Rag & Bone Featherweight Cam Full Straight Jeans

White jeans don’t have to be worn in casual settings only. Make yours feel more elegant with a tuxedo-style blazer, silk camisole, and smoking slippers. A structured top-handle bag adds a polished finishing touch, taking the look effortlessly from desk to dinner.

Simkhai Norah Satin Double-Breasted Blazer

Gucci Horsebit Duomo Small Top-Handle Bag

Dip your toes into the season’s brightest color trends by putting a bright button-down shirt with otherwise simple styling pieces, such as a white T-shirt and straight-leg jeans. A leather belt and high-vamp shoes provide pared-back elegance.

French girls know how to layer the je ne sais quoi way, and denim is often the base. White jeans get a Parisian twist when paired with not one but three fall jackets. After all, temperatures are always unpredictable. A chore coat, plaid blazer, and windbreaker jacket can all be part of your layering essentials.

Nothing is more classically cool than wearing black and white during fall and winter. Bring your baggy white jeans into the winter months with a black turtleneck cardigan and sleek textural accessories—an embossed belt, suede flats, and a rich leather bag. Use this look as a base, then add a leather jacket or a tailored wool coat for extra warmth when needed.

#6900. Where Is Joran van der Sloot Now? The Truth Behind Netflix’s ‘Taken: Natalee Holloway’

The new documentary revisits the 2005 disappearance that gripped the world.

Taken: Natalee Holloway is a new three-part Netflix documentary about the disappearance of 18-year-old Natalee Holloway, who vanished during a trip to Aruba in 2005. The series revisits the case through the eyes of her mother, Beth, who has never spoken in such detail about her ordeal before, along with the people who spent years searching for answers.

One question has followed the case for more than two decades: What really happened to Natalee, and what became of Joran van der Sloot, the man who admitted killing her but was never actually punished for it?

Joran van der Sloot is a Dutch man who was 17 when Natalee Holloway disappeared during what should have been a celebratory holiday with friends; he was one of the last people known to have seen her.

Natalee had traveled from Alabama to Aruba with classmates to celebrate their high school graduation, and was due to fly home on May 30, 2005. She was last seen leaving a bar in Oranjestad with van der Sloot and two other young men, brothers Deepak and Satish Kalpoe. All three were questioned and arrested during the investigation, but none was charged in connection with her disappearance at the time.

Natalee’s body has never been found, but van der Sloot has confessed to her murder.

Yes. In 2023, van der Sloot admitted that he killed Natalee in Aruba. According to a transcript of his account, he said they were walking on a beach when she rejected his advances. He said he kicked her and hit her with a cinder block, then pushed her body into the sea.

The confession came as part of a U.S. case against van der Sloot. He had tried to extort money from Natalee's mother, Beth Holloway, by promising to reveal the location of her daughter’s remains and reveal to her what had happened.

He pleaded guilty to extortion and wire fraud and was sentenced to 20 years in prison.

However, van der Sloot was never convicted of Natalee’s murder. The U.S. case was about extortion and wire fraud, and he has not been charged with her killing. So although he confessed, it is more accurate to say he admitted to killing Natalee than to describe him as a convicted murderer (at least in the case of Natalee).

Van der Sloot is in prison in Peru, where he is serving a 28-year sentence for the 2010 murder of Peruvian student Stephany Flores Ramírez. His U.S. sentence runs at the same time as his Peruvian sentence.

As of August 2026, he was reported to be held at Challapalca prison in southern Peru. He had asked to be moved, alleging threats from other inmates; reports said a judge still needed to rule on his request.

Stephany Flores Ramírez was a 21-year-old Peruvian business student. In May 2010, she was found dead in a hotel room in Lima, after checking in with Joran van der Sloot.

Van der Sloot was arrested in Chile and taken back to Peru. He initially confessed to killing Stephany, but later tried to withdraw his confession—however, a court ruled that it could be used as evidence.

In January 2012, he was sentenced to 28 years in prison for her murder and ordered to pay compensation to Stephany’s family.

The timing was especially eerie, as it is believed that Stephany was killed on the fifth anniversary of Natalee's disappearance. Although her case was separate, van der Sloot's arrest put him back in the spotlight and brought fresh attention to the questions that still surrounded Natalee's disappearance.

In 2023, van der Sloot admitted to killing her too, although he has never been convicted of Natalee's murder.

#6897. Demi Moore Takes Denim-on-Denim Dressing to the Next Level in a GapStudio Dress at the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards

A very American look for an American fashion ceremony

From hot-pink Matières Fécales ballgowns to iridescent Gucci dresses bursting with feathers and completely see-through chainmail Dolce & Gabbana slips, Demi Moore has never been afraid to take risks on the red carpet. So, when the Council of Fashion Designers of America (CFDA) hosted the 2026 Fashion Awards in New York City on Monday night (colloquially referred to as “the Oscars of American fashion”), it was no surprise to see Moore turning heads yet again.

With her look, the Oscar nominee took denim dressing to the next level, in a layered GapStudio dress designed by one of the night’s honorees: Zac Posen.

Since February 2024, Posen has acted as the creative director and executive vice president of Gap Inc., and the American brand for which he serves at the helm was recognized with the Innovation Award last night.

Posen’s experimental approach to the classic American brand was on full display when Moore arrived in a dress that looked like it was made out of nearly ten different pairs of blue jeans.

Composed of a dark-wash denim, the halter-style design featured a split-hem skirt that pooled on the ground, while the form-fitting bodice was made of stacks and stacks of material that emulated the waistbands of jeans. A line of buttons dotted the center, while up top, a final layer folded over to create a subtle sweetheart neckline.

For another denim touch, Moore carried a flap bag that even came equipped with a jean-fabric handle, designed by the brand’s executive director of accessories, Reed Krakoff. Finally, she completed the fit with tan suede pumps that peeked out from under the skirt of her dress, and a set of diamond earrings from Hassanzadeh.

Over the years, we’ve seen plenty of stars pull off denim-on-denim fits, but never with something quite like this.

Both feel so good and have made my skin have that just-got-a-facial glow.”—BG

#6892. The 7 Best Nail Trends to Try Right Now

From “Winter Wine” to “Tiger’s Eye,” these looks are perfect for colder months ahead

It’s a little too early for holiday nail art manicures, and maybe even too late for a fall palette of maples, golds, and pumpkin polish shades. So, what to do in the weeks of November ahead when it comes to your nail look?

“November calls for rich, warming shades such as chocolate brown, mocha, espresso, and caramel, alongside deep cherry, oxblood, and olive green,” says Townhouse founder Juanita Huber-Millet. “Our Flawless collection captures the season beautifully, with coffee-inspired shades like Chai Latte and Mocha, and timeless reds such as Red Apple and Cherry for an effortlessly chic fall manicure.”

Huber-Millet also recommends burgundy, classic red, and deep plum for a timeless winter shade. “This season, expect modern twists with glossy chrome and cat-eye finishes, colored French tips and delicate gems that add a festive touch without compromising on sophistication,” the professional adds.

Below, discover our edit of the best nail trends and colors to wear this November and beyond, including gemstone-inspired cat-eye shades and modern, high-shine neutrals to suit any occasion.

A rich, high-shine black-brown shade is the new classic color for the season. The key is to opt out of milkier shades and lean into depth, with crisp, clean cuticles and a generously glossy top coat.

Dress up purple and burgundy shades with subtle polka dots, embracing the year’s most playful nail art trend with a sophisticated, grown-up edge. The key is to keep the dots spacious and minimal.

A clean, black manicure is as much a timeless winter staple as a gray layering piece or a pair of leather boots. The key is to keep your nails short and smart with a soft-square shape, and pay extra attention to hydrating and tidying your cuticles.

Trade cow print and leopard spots for a more elevated, minimal zebra print this November. The best method is to put a playful twist on the classic French tip manicure, using fine lines to create subtle definition.

November’s take on the burgundy manicure is the darkest, glossiest yet. Pair deep, shiny pinotage tones with a classic shape, like almond or soft-square, and embrace purple-leaning hues over classic reds.

“Chrome and cat-eye finishes are key trends this winter,” says Huber-Millet. “At Townhouse, we’re seeing brown chrome become a firm favorite, combining rich, chocolatey tones with a luminous finish for a manicure that feels luxurious, contemporary, and perfectly suited to the season.”

The result? A multidimensional finish evocative of the classic tiger’s-eye gemstone.

Skip natural pink chrome and cat-eye this season and embrace rich, dark bases, from black to brown to burgundy. For a wintry twist, opt for a reflective blue or silver sheer chrome that allows the depth to shine through while highlighting its unique shift.

Every story we publish has been thoroughly researched and vetted by our team of beauty editors and industry experts.

#6891. The 2026 Esquire Cologne Awards

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Whether you’re looking to make a big impression on a special occasion or just searching for a great everyday scent, these winners have you covered.

There’s never been a better time to be a guy who wants to smell good. The men’s fragrance market is booming, the scents are bolder than ever, and you can shop for pretty much anything you could possibly imagine—from long-lasting extracts for a big night out to easy-to-wear options that’ll fit right in at the office—with ease.

The only problem with all that abundance is the project of narrowing things down to a manageable number of scents that might work for you. Which is where we come in. This year, the team here at Esquire decided to go big for our annual Cologne Awards. Our fragrance experts called in hundreds of new releases over the course of the year, narrowing them down through extensive testing and internal debate before each creating their own short list of winners.

That’s when things got really interesting. Round by round, the Esquire Cologne Awards committee met to discuss potential winners, spritzing and testing again and again before finally producing the list you see here. These are the 40 best scents of the year. You might not connect with every one of them—but if you can’t find something you love here, well, good luck out there.

This has become one of our daily wears—and for good reason. It’s going to please pretty much anyone. Spicy and aromatic, this cologne is vanilla and amber forward with discernible notes of Iris germanica and lavender giving it a more floral edge. The result is sensual and soft but never unmasculine, thanks to the aromatic freshness. Plus, it comes packaged in a giant flask. There’s nothing manlier than having a giant flask (literally) of cologne behind your bathroom mirror.

Leather lovers, this one is for you. Tom Ford’s newest Ombré Leather Reserve blends notes of earthy, black leather with silver juniper and sandalwood, creating a balanced, rooted, yet refreshing take on Ford’s classic Ombré Leather eau de parfum. Inspired by the great American West and the mountains and deserts that constitute it, this scent is a perfect balance between refinement and rugged masculinity.

If you were around in the ’90s and spent some time at the mall, you probably remember Gap Dream. It was the most iconic scent of the original lineup, which was discontinued in the early aughts. But Gap has given the people what they want: a relaunch of its beloved fragrance line. Dream remains the hero of the collection, fresh and clean like a brand-new white T-shirt, with bright citrus, soft florals, and botanical musk.

Noble but knows how to fuck. A raw oud with fresh sensuality. The peach is jam-like, adding a softness to the suede accord. Velvet Mood is a leather fragrance with added warmth, texture, and juicy sweetness. (It recalls the intimate, sexual encounter in Call Me by Your Name. The one with the peach. You know it.) It’s captivating and aura-maxxing, not a blind buy for most. But we’re still on the side of biting the peach.

This cologne has one job, and that job is beach. It’s salty, woody, and as fresh as anything else we smelled this year. From the blond driftwood to the beach rose, coconut water, and sea salt, all the notes are reminiscent of tropical paradise. It may not be summer anymore, but the perfect summer scent has landed. Now that it’s in your possession, you can turn any day into a beach day.

Though it is totally within the Ranger Station wheelhouse of western-inspired cowpoke smells, it’s a new earthy floral. The easy choice would have been to mix sandalwood and ambergris with whiskey and call it another saloon smell. Instead the Ranger Station team gave it an iris root heart—soft and floral without losing its edge. It’s not betting the farm and drinking away the loss. It’s betting the farm, losing it, and stepping out those double swing doors to see a sunrise and smell a new dewy and green morning. You lost it all, but that was yesterday. Keep moving forward and the next win can’t be too far down the road.

It took four years to get right and was, according to the company, the last thing on which Giorgio Armani put his personal stamp of approval. And it’s not what you might expect if you’re thinking of the aquatic, mineral freshness of something like Acqua di Giò. I Will is a different animal—all wood and vanilla with bergamot to keep things from getting too dark and dense. It’s rich and modern and exactly what people will be talking about 20 years from now when they’re discussing the Italian fashion house’s other iconic scents.

Soft and sensual, Musc Pallida lingers like a whisper on your skin. Powdery iris and warm musk are the main components here, and they work together seamlessly. It’s light and airy but doesn’t fade quickly, and it smells good on pretty much anyone. Don’t go into Musc Pallida expecting your typical manly musk fragrance—it’s much more elegant and intimate.

Earthy sweetness, earthly delights. This fragrance from Byredo is sweet without ever venturing into gourmand territory. Even with its Madagascar vanilla and vanilla liquor accord at the base, it is punctuated with fougères and florals that give it complexity without steering it into being a spice bomb or a cognac cocktail. Instead it has a bright greenness and soft musk that can see the light of day and what’s to come. Simply fresh, refined with some sweetness—for a beautiful day.

A sophisticated blend of cinnamon, sage, and bergamot gives this fragrance a warm, spicy character. Cinnamon fragrances tend to be overpowering and reserved for nighttime, but this scent feels sophisticated and is a great daytime spice since the spicy top notes are balanced by a vanilla and praline base. It’s an elegant take on a spice-forward scent and a Nordstrom exclusive, which makes sense since Parfums de Marly is one of the retailer’s top-selling brands.

Creed’s Iris Debonair—a totally new offering, not a new expression—walks a wonderful line. It’s very gentlemanly with its cool and classic fougère heart. The opening and its transition into the middle is a lot of bergamot, absinthe, and lavender, making it seem like an expected (albeit exceptional) barbershop scent. But as it settles, iris starts to come to the forefront. It’s not the old-fashioned iris of Green Irish Tweed but a softer one that pairs well with a soft ambrette dry down. It has a true masculine and a true feminine side, and neither controls your lasting memory of the scent. Totally captivating.

Citrus and amber are not natural bedfellows, but this scent—a more highly concentrated version of the former Le Gemme Tygar—makes a very strong case for their marriage. It’s bright and grapefruit-y in one instant and then rich and woody in another. The more you smell it, the more the moniker starts to make sense. It is, after all, named after tigereye, a gemstone revered not for its sameness but for the striking impression that its contrasting bands of color create.

Fig fragrances are having a moment, but this new Granado EDP blows all other fig scents out of the water. Candied green fig reveals rich, jammy notes that are sweet but not in the typical syrupy way; it’s a lush, even fresh kind of sweet. Once you get hints of sweet dates, florals, and woods, it all combines to create a beautifully creamy gourmand.

It opens fruity and floral. A burst of raspberry gives way to peony, geranium, jasmine, magnolia. That might sound a little tough for some guys—but remember it’s just the beginning. Those notes soon give way to a base of deep, powdery musks. It’s not your typical masculine fragrance; it’s something a little more delicate and, though the scent is rooted in centuries of Chinese tradition, modern. We’d encourage you to give it a try. You might just find a new daily driver.

Before you spritz this woody scent, you have to admire the artistry of the bottle. Mind Games is known for its chess-inspired bottles, and Knight’s Tour is a great example of that. The scent inside is a sophisticated blend of lavender and saffron with spicy accents up top and smooth woods at the base. It’s a great go-to option for every day.

One Esquire editor made Ripe her scent of the summer after it launched in the spring. The reason? Its perfect balance of warm florals, fresh fruity notes, soft woods, and musks is lush and enticing. Have you ever thought about how notes of lychee, watermelon sorbet, green leaves, rose water, and creamy sandalwood work together? Spoiler alert: It’s pretty awesome.

Ojai is like waking up in a luxury hotel in California, sunlight peeking through the shades as fruity and floral notes dance around the room. Juicy, ripe mango and honey-like saffron are at the heart of this fragrance, giving it a sweet yet elevated quality. But it also has an earthy, amber-y depth to it, thanks to patchouli and frankincense at the base.

Put rhubarb in a fragrance and we’re there. Demon Dancer takes the tartness of rhubarb and blackcurrant and adds bright citrus, fresh peppermint, and radiant orange blossom for an exciting opening before it settles into a woody base of amber, musk, and tonka bean. When the cool, fresh top and heart notes reveal the warm base, it really is something to take in.

Imagine that feeling when you’re walking around a smog-choked city and find an unexpected oasis. It’s fresh, green, but with just that tiniest hint of something darker and deeper. That’s this scent. It’s wood and pepper and greenery and a reminder that even in the midst of urban sprawl, we can always find a way back to nature.

The Dolce & Gabbana classic is now available as an alcohol-free perfume gel, making it a travel-friendly option for sensitive skin. The curved metal applicator makes it easy to apply the fragrance directly to pulse points, while the pocket-sized packaging is perfect for on-the-go touch-ups. The gel formula also works as a great base for layering if you already own the original fragrance and want to extend the wear time of the cult-favorite citrus scent.

#6890. Designers Say These Are the 10 Best Antiques to Find at Estate Sales

Thrift stores and flea markets are popular spots for finding unusual vintage pieces, but they’re not the only options. Estate sales let you browse antiques in the very homes where they’ve been used and collected over the years. And since many items haven’t passed through a dealer, they may cost less than similar finds at an antique shop.

That combination makes estate sales a favorite among interior designers. They’re a source of characterful pieces for both client projects and personal collections. Krystal Reinhard, founder and principal designer of Old Soul Design Studio, likes being able to see items in the settings where they’ve long belonged. “It makes it easier to spot quality, scale, and patina,” she says.

For Valerie Helgeson, founder and lead designer at Design Directions, the appeal of estate sales is the fast-paced nature of the hunt. “There’s an immediacy to them that I like. You have to make a decision and trust your instincts,” she shares.

Whether you’re after a bargain, an authentic antique, or the fun of finding an unexpected treasure, estate sales are worth exploring. Ahead, interior designers reveal the 10 antiques they always look for—and explain why each belongs on your estate sale wish list.

Paige Loperfido, founder and principal designer of Decor & More Design Studio, is always game to grab hand-painted porcelain, midcentury ceramics, and antique glassware, like ice buckets and goblets.

“These collectibles bring color, pattern, and personality to kitchens and dining rooms,” she says. “Beyond their practical use, they easily transition into art, with vintage plates mounted on walls for a dynamic display, or grouped on open shelves. In dining spaces, glassware and tableware act as statement pieces, highlighting one-of-a-kind treasures that feel timeless, sophisticated, and thoughtfully curated, adding both charm and character to special gatherings.”

Reinhard calls antique candlestick holders “an instant layer,” with their warmth, height, and “a little romance with a surprisingly big impact for a small spend.” She adds that she often styles them as matched pairs on mantels and consoles, or as loose trios at varying heights on dining tables and coffee tables.

Going into estate sales with an open mind, Helgeson typically finds herself buying something she “could have never imagined looking for.” But frequently, one item tops her wish list, and that’s copper kitchenware. She displays these antique pieces in kitchens alongside other estate sale–found items, such as turned-wood bowls, old cutting boards, and bread bowls.

Pottery is a designer darling, since these pieces add texture and even imperfection (in a good way) to spaces, as Reinhard puts it. She tracks down pottery at estate sales, then showcases it on open shelving, sideboards, and tablescapes. She prefers to pair pottery with “something refined,” like brass, stone, or glass, to keep the mix elevated.

Loperfido’s favorite estate sale gems are what she calls “culturally rich pieces” that have been collected by homeowners from around the world. Think: Chinese antiques like foo dogs and ginger jars, and vintage ceramics such as majolica fish candlesticks, figurative pieces, or midcentury glazed pottery.

Oftentimes, she says that these particular pieces “showcase incredible craftsmanship and character,” adding that they’re perfect to layer on surfaces throughout the house, from bookshelves to nightstands to end tables.

At estate sales, Helgeson is scanning the spaces for “furniture with a strong silhouette.” Acting as anchors in rooms, Helgeson believes in accessorizing the rest of these spaces with vintage finds, which she says makes even new pieces appear like collected treasures.

“Vintage rugs bring color, pattern, and age instantly,” Reinhard remarks. She decorates with them “everywhere”: in powder rooms, mudrooms, and under seating areas to anchor the furniture and add depth.

“Original art can feel inaccessible at gallery pricing, but estate sales are one of the best places to find something truly special for a fraction of the cost,” Reinhard points out. She goes for single statement pieces, small collected clusters, or creates a full gallery wall that combines vintage art with modern pieces and personal photos.

Continually on the lookout for leather-bound collections and cocktail books, Reinhard snaps up antique books at estate sales.

“They’re one of the easiest ways to add personality and story—especially art, history, and travel titles that feel timeless and intentional,” she says. “I use them everywhere—stacked on coffee tables, tucked onto consoles, layered into shelves, or used as risers for objects to create height and visual rhythm.”

Loperfido says that these “finely detailed etched artworks” are often overlooked at estate sales, but they can instantly add sophistication and historical texture to any space.

“Small but rich in visual storytelling, they work beautifully against neutral or patterned backgrounds,” she notes.

#6888. Meet Melat Kiros, the 29-Year-Old Bringing Gen Z Politics to Congress

After losing her law-firm job over her stance on Gaza protests, she turned generational frustration into a historic win.

In 2023, Melat Kiros was fired. She was 26 and worked at a major New York law firm, but she refused to condemn an open letter addressed to dozens of law firms that had urged law school deans to crack down on antisemitism on university campuses, at a time when students were protesting the slaughter in Gaza. Kiros condemned antisemitism, but warned law firms, including the one she worked for, against confusing “bigotry with the geopolitical question of Israel’s legitimacy.” This cost her her job.

She, however, was not discouraged, as she belongs to a generation that is no longer willing to set aside its values for the sake of a career. “The right to question not only the legitimacy of a government but also its actions is the bedrock principle of a true democracy,” she said of her decision. Since then, Kiros has devoted herself more intensely to politics, and yesterday, in the midterm elections, she won as Colorado’s representative to the House of Representatives. Before her, only one other Gen Z representative, Democrat Maxwell Frost of Florida, had made it to Parliament in 2022, but she became the first Gen Z woman in Congress.

Melat Kiros was born in Addis Ababa, Ethiopia, but moved to Denver with her family when she was still a baby and grew up there. She graduated from Washington College in Maryland in 2018, where she studied political science and economics, before earning a law degree from the University of Notre Dame in 2022. Today she is a lawyer and is pursuing a doctorate in public policy. In the meantime, however, she also works as a barista. It is not easy to balance a political career with her studies, but it is worth it.

After being fired, Kiros returned to Colorado and, in 2024, joined the election campaign of Democratic congressional candidate John Padora as communications director. It was excellent training for her, so much so that, two years later, she decided to run for Congress herself. On her side, she has strong political positions—she supports universal health care and child care, but also more politically controversial issues, such as abolishing Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) and ending U.S. military aid to Israel. Her ideals openly align with her actions, and in fact during the election campaign she refused funding from corporate PACs and pro-Israel groups, while also sharply criticizing her Democratic primary opponent Diana DeGette, who had instead accepted donations from defense contractors as well as energy and pharmaceutical companies.

It is clear that Kiros's mark is different: She shows us what Gen Z can and will be able to do when it is given space. In this sense, her election also fits well into the climate of student protests, in Europe and around the world, by a generation that wants to make its voice heard. Yet it is still not easy: According to research by Future Caucus, more than 100 Generation Z candidates ran in the 2026 midterm elections for Congress, but the study found that young candidates were less likely to make it to the general election than other age groups, with only 24 of them appearing on the November ballots.

Kiros also had to face paternalism and dismissive attitudes from those who considered her unsuitable and inexperienced. “Now is not the time to gamble and send somebody with no experience to Washington,” DeGette had said during the primaries. Kiros, however, turned it into a strength, centering her congressional candidacy precisely on the theme of generational change. She emphasized socialist issues dear to younger people, such as affordability, social policies, climate change, countering the influence of multinational corporations, and support for students, families, and workers.

“People, I think, were really eager to see someone who is younger, who has lived experience with the economy that we're living through right now, the climate crisis that we are going to have to deal with the consequences of,” she said in an interview. For her, “socialism is about making it clear that we have basic needs for every single human being,” and she has promised to fight for this.

She also knows she has a lot to learn, but that does not stop her from walking through the doors of Congress with her head held high, without being overly intimidated by traditions and alliances. “Young people understand that we are in the fight of our lives,” she said. “We need to have leaders that are going to be fighting for the kind of bold policies that actually get something done, and they’re refusing to settle or wait for our turn or ask for permission.”

#6887. Dior and Harper’s Bazaar Host a Screening of the New Richard Avedon Documentary in Los Angeles

With Emma Watson, Gia Coppola, and more in attendance

From a Paris Fashion Week toast to a New York City launch party, Dior and Harper’s Bazaar have been honoring the legacy of fashion photographer Richard Avedon these past few weeks. This series of intimate events has been tied to the launch of a new coffee-table book titled Richard Avedon: The Harper’s Bazaar Years, as well as a documentary film, Avedon. Now, the festivities have traveled to Los Angeles.

Avedon, who passed away in 2004 at the age of 81, was one of the most influential fashion photographers of the 20th century. In 1944, at just 21 years old, he started his career at Harper’s Bazaar under the guidance of then-editor-in-chief Carmel Snow, and he spent the next two-plus decades defining the creative vision of the publication.

Richard Avedon: The Harper’s Bazaar Years (available now) explores this artistic period by compiling rarely seen photographs, a never-before-published account from Avedon himself, a foreword by editor-in-chief Samira Nasr, and an essay by Bazaar contributing editor Derek C. Blasberg. The new documentary, meanwhile, offers a look through the Avedon archives from a different lens. It was directed by Academy Award–winning filmmaker Ron Howard (Apollo 13, A Beautiful Mind, The Da Vinci Code) and produced by Imagine Documentaries. It will be released on Netflix on November 11.

Focusing on the film, Dior and Blasberg hosted a private screening of Avedon at the William Morris Endeavor headquarters in L.A. Guests such as Emma Watson, Gia Coppola, Lauren Hutton, Devon Lee Carlson, and more gathered to watch the film before heading to the House of Dior in Beverly Hills for a cocktail reception.

Ahead, we offer an intimate look inside the event.

#6883. Which Couples Broke Up (and Who Got Married) on ‘Love Is Blind’ Season 11?

A brand-new batch of aspiring reality-star singles gathered in Boston for the annual social experiment known as Love Is Blind, and the final installment of season 11 is officially out on Netflix. This week’s finale saw multiple couples break up and just one pair make it down the aisle—here’s where things stand going into the reunion!

Despite managing to work through some of their communication issues, Aneha’s parents refused to attend her wedding to Cam—and her sister made it abundantly clear she wasn’t on board either. Ultimately, Aneha turned down Cam at the altar, but also seemed to want to stay together? Meanwhile, Cam was less sure about going forward in the relationship, so guess we’ll see where they stand at the reunion! (Spoilers this way if you can’t wait.)

Currently: still split...but kinda vibing?

Jax might have ghosted Stew immediately after their engagement, but they reunited with the cast at a bar and spent the entire time joking around with each other at a table and watching the mess unfold. I swear, they had chemistry. Weird chemistry, but still chemistry! Though, for what it’s worth, Jax insists nothing is going on.

#6876. All the Historic Firsts From the 2026 Midterms

The latest U.S. election saw history being made in states like Texas, California, Georgia, and more

On Tuesday, midterm general elections were held in the United States to determine all 435 seats in the House of Representatives, as well as 35 of the 100 seats in the Senate. Meanwhile, key governor races were held across 34 states, like New York, Texas, California, Florida, and Michigan.

These specific elections take place every four years during the middle of a presidential term, acting as a measurement of how happy voters are with the president’s current performance. More importantly, they help determine which side of the political spectrum will control Congress, the body of government that creates and passes legislation.

This time, Democrats ended up winning overwhelmingly, gaining seven seats in the Senate and 25 seats in the House, meaning the party is now in control of Congress and has significant power to buck many of President Donald Trump’s measures.

It was a joyous night for the left, it was also a night of many historic firsts for a U.S. election. Keisha Lance Bottoms became the first Black woman to ever be elected governor in American history, while Deb Haaland is the first Native American. Chris Pappas became the first openly gay man ever elected to the Senate, while Abdul El-Sayed is the first Muslim candidate to join it.

Others made history in their specific states. Xavier Becerra is California’s first elected Latino governor. David Crowley is Wisconsin’s first-ever Black governor. Amy Acton is the first woman to ever become governor in Ohio. And that’s just the beginning.

Ahead, we break down all the historic firsts that happened during the 2026 U.S. midterm general elections.

Bottoms becomes the first Black candidate and first woman to ever be elected governor in Georgia. She also makes history as the first Black woman to ever be elected governor in the United States.

El-Sayed will become the first Muslim member of Senate in U.S. history, after he won his election in Michigan.

As a current House of Representatives member for New Hampshire, Pappas is the first openly gay man to ever represent the state in Congress. Now, with his Senate seat win, Pappas becomes the first openly gay man to ever be elected to the U.S. Senate.

Haaland is set to become the first Native American female governor in U.S. history. Previously, she was one of the first two indigenous women to ever be elected to Congress in New Mexico.

Acton becomes the first woman and first Jewish person to ever be elected governor in Ohio, defeating Republican candidate Vivek Ramaswamy. Previously, she served as the director of the Ohio Department of Health, and was also the first woman to hold that post.

Attorney and politician Xavier Becerra will become the first elected Latino governor of California. The last Latino to serve as governor in the state was José Antonio Romualdo Pacheco back in 1875, who ascended to the role when then-governor Newton Booth was elected to the U.S. Senate.

Montgomery becomes the first transgender state lawmaker in Alabama, in the state’s House District 25 seat.

Crowley is the first Black candidate ever elected governor of Wisconsin. Previously, he served as the executive of Milwaukee County and was the first Black person to serve in that position, as well as the youngest, as he was elected at age 33.

Hinojosa becomes the first Hispanic candidate ever elected governor in the state of Texas, and she also becomes the first Democrat to hold the post since Ann Richards won in 1990.

Flanagan, who is a member of the White Earth Band of Ojibwe in Minnesota, becomes the first Native American woman elected to the Senate.

Ford becomes the first Black governor of Nevada, after previously serving as the first Black attorney general in the state.

Klobuchar becomes the first woman elected governor in the state of Minnesota.

Kiros becomes the first Gen Z woman elected to Congress, as part of Colorado’s first congressional district.

With her election to the Washington state legislature, Scott becomes the first out Black trans state lawmaker in the country.

#6871. 13 Things You Should Toss ASAP If You’ve Lived in Your Home for More Than 5 Years

You'll feel a wave of relief once you do it.

It may seem like you just moved into your new home or apartment yesterday. But in a flash, five years have passed and you’re out of storage and feeling overwhelmed. Most of us know it doesn’t take long for our belongings to pile up.

“By year five, the ‘endless storage’ honeymoon is over,” says Ann Lightfoot, cofounder, DDH Home Organizing & Move Management. “Your stuff has multiplied, your square footage hasn’t, and you now know how you actually live in your space.”

But now is the ideal time for a thoughtful edit. “Taking stock after five years gives us a chance to see what's actually taking up space, what we're still using, and what we've simply gotten used to storing,” says Laura Kinsella, founder of Urban OrgaNYze. “Our careers, families, routines, and interests can change dramatically, but the things we've accumulated don't automatically change with us.”

Decluttering also enhances good design. “To me, a beautifully designed home isn't about how much you can fit into it. It's about being intentional with what you choose to keep,” says Kerrie Kelly, NKBA, FASID, CEO and creative director of Kerrie Kelly Studio, and NKBA 2026 National Board Chair. “Editing isn't about deprivation; it's about making room for what you actually love and use.”

If you don’t know where to begin, start small. “Edit by category rather than trying to tackle the entire house in a weekend,” says Kelly. “Start with one drawer, one cabinet, or one closet. Take everything out, clean the space, and put back only what deserves to be there. It's amazing how quickly those small decisions transform the way a home feels. Empty space is a luxury, too!”

Ahead, here’s what design and organizational experts say you should get rid of if you’ve been in your home five years or more.

“Five years of conferences, events, fundraisers, and corporate gifting can leave you with an impressive collection of tote bags, reusable water bottles, and branded merchandise you never actually set out to own,” says Kinsella. Keep what you genuinely like and use, and pass along the extras.

Most manuals live online now, and that tangle of chargers likely powers nothing in your house. Recycle them responsibly, says Lightfoot.

“Threadbare towels, mismatched sheets, and extra bedding you never reach for take up valuable storage space,” says Kelly. “Keep a thoughtfully curated collection of beautiful, functional linens and recycle the worn-out ones where possible.”

“If your spin shoes haven't been touched since lockdown and the exercise bike has become ‘the chair’ in your bedroom, you may be dedicating valuable space to a workout routine that's no longer part of your life,” says Kinsella. Sell or donate equipment you no longer use so you have room for the activities you truly enjoy now.

Craft kits, furniture you planned to refinish, the bread maker and so on: “If someday hasn’t arrived in five years, give yourself permission to let it go, guilt-free,” says Lightfoot.

“Those little objects that have been sitting on a shelf for years can become visual clutter,” says Kelly. “I love a meaningful collection, but every piece should earn its place. Sometimes removing three things makes the one beautiful object remaining look even better.”

“That rug or wall art you swore would work ‘somewhere’ has spent five years leaning against a wall. It’s time to release it to a home where it fits,” says Lightfoot.

Hang on to a small labeled paint sample for touch-ups—and maybe one tile. But the rest, including the dried-up products or the five half-empty cans in the garage or basement, can go to your town’s hazardous waste drop-off, says Lightfoot.

This is a no-judgment zone: “If the avocado slicer, specialty pan, or oversized appliance hasn't made an appearance in years, it probably doesn't deserve prime real estate in your kitchen,” says Kelly. Ditto for duplicate kitchen tools.

“Whether it's a juicer, red light mask, or massage tool, we've all had that new wellness routine we're completely committed to for a few weeks before life gets busy and the habit quietly falls away,” says Kinsella. “The problem is that the gadget doesn't disappear when the enthusiasm does, and five years later, you may have an entire cabinet devoted to routines that lasted a month.” It’s fine to try something new to learn it wasn't for you, but you don't have to keep storing the equipment forever.

Maybe it's an extra accent chair that collects clothes or a side table that's always in the way. Just because something fits physically doesn't mean it belongs. Give your rooms space to breathe and your furniture room to shine, says Kelly.

Purge the old sunscreen, crusty nail polish, expired makeup, shampoo you don’t like, and half-used cleaners that have piled up in your bathroom. Toss them safely and enjoy the instant breathing room, says Lightfoot.

“If you've lived in your home for five years and still have boxes you never unpacked, it's a pretty good indication that you haven't needed most of what's inside on a regular basis,” says Kinsella. “Open the box, decide what's worth keeping, and find those belongings a proper home so you're not continuing to live around a move that’s already happened.”

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.
